# Build plan

The live status of the build. Update this file as steps close — it is the one
place that answers "where are we?" after a context clear.

**Architecture:** [`docs/adr/ADR-035`](adr/ADR-035-target-architecture-postgres-react-native.md).
Where this file and the ADR disagree, the ADR wins and this file is the bug.

**New here, or not a developer?** [`docs/HANDBOOK.md`](HANDBOOK.md) explains the
tooling, the working loop, and where Claude is likely to be wrong.

**Stack:** Postgres (Supabase) + React Native (Expo). Mexico-based small retailers,
MXN, IVA. Spanish module names are the domain vocabulary: Comprar (buy), Vender
(sell), Productos (catalog), Proveedores (providers), Desperdicio (waste),
Números (reports).

**Not the stack:** Power Apps Canvas + Dataverse. That era stopped 2026-08-14 and
lives in [`archive/power-platform/`](../archive/power-platform/README.md), excluded
from the knowledge graph. Nothing there describes the system being built.

---

## Position

### ⛔ DECISIONS OWED BY THE OWNER

⚠️ **THIS BLOCK EXISTS SO NOBODY HAS TO REMEMBER ANYTHING.** Every session reads
`## Position` first, and the working prompt ends by asking what decision is needed — so a
decision parked here is re-offered **every session, automatically**, until it is ruled on.
⚠️ **It is the ONE home for open owner decisions**, and `docs/checks/plan-handover.sh`
refuses a second one, refuses a row that does not say what it blocks, and ⚠️⚠️ **refuses to
let a blocked task be marked as the next task.**

| **What a fonda and a neighbour see of each other, and how each rates the other — five questions** (`10.5b-ii-a`, drawn 2026-11-10: https://claude.ai/artifact/RsL656sDovJ5ng1Mw8XEhQ — a day at a fonda, both phones to try, the conflicts, what other apps do, and each question's recommendation, reason, argument against and cost to reverse) | `10.5b-ii-b` — his picks built: the names and the ratings, their database and both apps' additions | **Q1, a name:** the kitchen she reserved at sees *Ana R.* (first name and initial) on that reservation, on the day she collects it and never after; Itacate tells her before she reserves; no name on her account → the code alone. **Recommended: yes.** **Q2, her visits there:** *Primera vez aquí* / *Ha venido 4 veces* beside the name — pickups at that kitchen only, never her no-shows, never other kitchens. **Recommended: yes.** **Q3, how she rates the fonda:** one tap of stars after *Ya lo tengo* (questions 7–10), then tags from a closed list, and a note *solo lo lee Wera*; the kitchen's page shows its stars and top tags from five ratings; the fonda sees her own the next day, mixed, never who, with *Reportar*; Itacate asks her once to accept the terms before her first rating (Google counts reviews as user content). **Recommended: yes.** **Q4, whether the fonda rates her:** no stars — the clock already records whether she came, *Hubo un problema* covers the rest. **Recommended: no stars.** **Q5, her record across kitchens, and a fonda cancelling on it:** not in the pilot — her express consent (LFPDPPP Art. 35; LFPC Art. 76 Bis), LFPC Art. 58 on refusing a customer, a new account escapes it in a minute (`mailer_autoconfirm`), and a cancel needs a push to Itacate; after two weeks of real reservations (question 9) a session brings him the no-show numbers and, if they hurt, *trust that grows* (one reservation until her first pickup, three after; two *se liberó* in 30 days back to one). **Recommended: not in the pilot.** *"As recommended"* answers all five |

### ⏳ DATES OWED — what a calendar owes, and no person is holding

⚠️⚠️ **THIS BLOCK EXISTS BECAUSE THE DECISIONS BLOCK DID NOT COVER DATES, AND ON 2026-09-13
THERE WERE THREE LIVE ONES HELD ONLY BY PROSE.** A decision waits until someone rules; **a
date passes whether or not anyone looked**, and a missed reading is not recoverable by
trying harder afterwards — it is recoverable only by starting the clock again.

⚠️ **A DATE IS NOT A TASK.** It carries no next-task marker, it is not sized, and it must
never be what a cleared session "takes" — the next task is always in the tables below.

⚠️⚠️ **AND THIS BLOCK HAS TEETH THE DECISIONS BLOCK DOES NOT: `plan-handover.sh` FAILS once
a due date is in the PAST and its row is not ticked.** A red here blocks every merge, which
is deliberate and is the only reason a date survives a context clear — **and the exit is ten
seconds: tick the box and write what was read.** ⚠️ It fires only on dates **strictly
before** today (UTC), so a due-today row is never a false red on a timezone.

| Due | What | Why it cannot simply be moved | Done |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2026-10-04** | ⚠️⚠️ **RE-DEPLOY WERA TO THE IPHONE — THE SUCCESSOR TO THE 2026-09-27 ROW, AND IT NOW CARRIES THE PREREQUISITE THAT ROW DISCOVERED.** Profile `ad8112ec` expires **2026-10-04T14:28:16Z**, and when it does **the app stops launching in his hand** — measured on 2026-09-27, when he said *"I can't open the app in my phone, looks like the profile already expired"* within minutes of the moment. ⚠️⚠️ **THE TWO THINGS THAT MUST BE TRUE BEFORE A RENEWAL CAN WORK, BOTH LEARNED THE EXPENSIVE WAY ON 2026-09-27:** **(1) the build must run ON or AFTER the expiry** — `-allowProvisioningUpdates` REUSES a valid profile and RENEWS only an expired or missing one, which seven early re-deploys demonstrated; and **(2) an Apple ID must be signed into Xcode** (*Xcode → Settings → Accounts*), because renewing talks to Apple and reusing does not. **Check (2) BEFORE spending ten minutes on a build**: `ls ~/Library/Developer/Xcode/UserData/Provisioning\ Profiles/ \| wc -l` and `security find-identity -v -p codesigning`. ⚠️ **AND THE OWNER MUST TAP TRUST AFTERWARDS, EVERY TIME** — *Settings → General → VPN & Device Management* — because a new PROFILE requires it even when the certificate is unchanged. That is the 2026-09-27 correction and it is not optional. ⚠️ **A free personal team signs for seven days**, so this row will have a successor, and the 2026-10-13 reading below needs the last one to land inside the seven days ending on that date. ⚠️⚠️ **AND A THIRD PREREQUISITE WAS LEARNED ON 2026-09-27 BY `6a-ii-a`, WHICH BUILT SUCCESSFULLY AND COULD NOT INSTALL: THE PHONE HAS TO BE REACHABLE, AND THE ERROR FOR *not reachable* IS NOT THE ERROR FOR *locked*.** `devicectl` reads `unavailable` and every install answers **`com.apple.dt.CoreDeviceError 1011`** — *unable to locate a device matching the requested device identifier* — where the passcode answers `kAMDMobileImageMounterDeviceLocked` or `CoreDeviceError 3`. **A retry loop written for the lock spins on `1011` for ever**, which is what twenty attempts over five minutes demonstrated. ⚠️ **Check it BEFORE spending ten minutes on a build**: `xcrun devicectl list devices` must read `available (paired)`, and `ping iPhone-de-Bernie.coredevice.local` must answer | ⚠️⚠️ **THE APP STOPS LAUNCHING WHEN IT PASSES, AND HE IS USING IT DAILY.** ⚠️ The data container survives a re-install — proved three times — so the cost is the trust tap and ten minutes, not his session | ☑ **DONE LATE, 2026-10-09** — missed on the day (the `9i` session found the profile expired and not renewed). Re-deployed 2026-10-09 **as `mx.wera.app`**, `5R-a`'s id, after the owner confirmed nothing was recorded offline before the old build stopped opening. `app/ios` was regenerated by `expo prebuild --clean`. ✅ **The embedded profile now expires 2027-10-09**: it was signed by the paid individual account (team `RGWA74UM3G`), so **the seven-day re-deploy is retired** and this row has no successor. It installed over WiFi and launched without a Trust prompt. The old `mx.bserafin.wera` stays on the phone as a dead icon until he deletes it |
| **2026-10-13** | ⚠️⚠️ **`5a-iv-d` day-30 reading — ON ONE INSTRUMENT NOW, NOT TWO, AND THE OWNER TRADED THE OTHER ON 2026-09-22 KNOWING WHAT IT COST.** ***"Override anything related to the 30 day session check. I want to keep working from my device."*** **The iPhone half is spent**: he is using the app daily, so its session is refreshed on every open and it can no longer answer *how long does a session survive untouched*. ⚠️ **The reading is still worth taking and still free, because the second instrument was sealed for exactly this**: the AVD `wera-reading-5a-iv-d`, a **different Google account**, no provisioning clock, powered down since 2026-09-21 — which is why a second instrument existed at all. **A one-device reading is a weaker claim than a two-device one, and it is the claim this project now has.** ⚠️ The iPhone row above is no longer part of this measurement. ⚠️⚠️ **AND IT NO LONGER MEASURES THIRTY DAYS — SAY SO NOW RATHER THAN DISCOVER IT THEN.** The day-8 reading of 2026-09-21 refreshed both sessions by opening both apps, which is what that reading IS; so this one measures **22 days** from 2026-09-21, not 30 from day 0. ⚠️ **That is not a defect and the row is still worth taking** — C1.4's claim is unbounded, and 22 untouched days is a longer gap than 8 — but *"day 30"* is now a name rather than an interval, and a session reporting it as thirty days since sign-in would be stating something false | C1.4 claims persistence *"until an explicit log-out"*, which is unbounded; **eight days can only fail to disprove it.** Both devices are sealed already, so this costs a glance. ⚠️ **The iPhone half also needs the 2026-09-27 re-deploy to have been TRUSTED** — see the row above. ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE ANDROID HALF MAY REFUSE TO BOOT: the sealed AVD was hard-killed on 2026-09-21 and left `hardware-qemu.ini.lock` and `multiinstance.lock` behind** in `~/.android/avd/wera-reading-5a-iv-d.avd/`. A stale lock reads as *"another emulator instance is running"* on the next boot — **which is this date, the one day it is expensive.** ✅ **The fix is to delete those two files and boot again**; the app's data is on the disk image and is not affected. ⚠️ **Do NOT launch Wera to check — booting the emulator is safe, opening the app is the reading** ⚠️⚠️ **2026-10-13, 01:52 UTC (the `5R-d-ii` session): both stale locks were DELETED; the boot itself was REFUSED by the session's permission check, so the owner runs it.** **Boot it COLD — `emulator -avd wera-reading-5a-iv-d -no-snapshot-load -no-snapshot-save`.** Its quick-boot snapshot (`snapshots/default_boot`) is the 2026-09-13 seal: loading it could roll the disk back to the day-0 refresh token, which the day-8 reading already spent, and with reuse detection on that replay revokes the session — a false red that looks exactly like the answer. Its `ram.bin` is 0 bytes (the 09-21 hard kill), so it may not load at all, which is not a reason to risk it | ☑ **DONE 2026-10-13 04:36 UTC — THE SESSION SURVIVED.** The owner booted it cold from Terminal.app (the flags above). Before reading the screen, the instrument was checked over `adb`: the AVD named `wera-reading-5a-iv-d`, its clock equal to the host's to the second (`auto_time` 1), `mx.bserafin.wera` the top resumed activity. **Wera opened signed in, on Inicio** — *Cerrar sesión* and the shop's $11.60 on screen (`uiautomator` and a screencap). The disk image was last written 2026-09-21 11:54 CST, so the session sat untouched **about 21½ days** and was restored by opening the app alone. ⚠️ **A weaker reading than day 8's**: a screen, not the before/after diff of `last_sign_in_at`, because that reads the stored credentials. **No successor is scheduled**; the AVD is free |

▶️ **2026-11-10 — `10.5b-ii-a` DONE: WHAT A FONDA AND A NEIGHBOUR SEE OF EACH OTHER, AND HOW EACH RATES THE OTHER, ARE DRAWN FOR HIM — A DAY AT A FONDA, BOTH PHONES TO TRY, WHAT THE LAW AND THE STORES REQUIRE, WHAT OTHER APPS DO, AND FIVE QUESTIONS, EACH WITH A RECOMMENDATION. `10.5b-ii` SPLIT ON ITS GATE: HIS PICKS BUILT ARE `10.5b-ii-b`, WAITING ON THE FIVE ANSWERS. RESERVING ITSELF DEPENDS ON NONE OF THEM, SO `10.5b-iii` IS THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words:** none new — the row is his word of 2026-11-10.
**The estimate, written first:** `M`, as the row said — a page, his questions, the plan. What reading the code first found: **a neighbour's whole record is already in `0079`** (each reservation's moments, read by `reservation_state`), so a record is a function, not a table; **a name exists for most neighbours** — Itacate asks *Nombre* and *Apellido* at *Crear cuenta* (`itacate/src/auth/credentials.ts`), and Google's sign-in and Apple's first one give one; **the aviso promises the opposite of what he opened** — *ninguna cocina ni tienda sabe que entras a Itacate*, *no armamos perfiles … no tomamos decisiones automáticas sobre ti*; and **both apps' terms of use define no objectionable content, and no app asks anyone to accept them** (`site/terminos/cocina-e-itacate/`).
**Nothing left behind, read first:** no open pull request; `plan-handover.sh` 11 of 11; every migration to `0079` on the hosted project (`supabase migration list --linked`).
**What was made:** https://claude.ai/artifact/RsL656sDovJ5ng1Mw8XEhQ — (1) **a day at Doña Lupe's in seven moments**, each with what it shows: Ana reserves (no accept step, so whatever the fonda sees arrives after the plate is held), three people at the counter (where a name helps every day), someone never comes (already recorded by the clock), *Ya lo tengo* and a rating, Ana at another kitchen, a neighbour with three no-shows starting over with a new email, a rating in anger; (2) **two labs, Itacate and Wera Cocina side by side**: five things Lupe could see of a neighbour, with what Itacate tells her before she reserves; five ways a neighbour rates a fonda and two ways a fonda rates a neighbour, on the rating sheet and the fonda's screen; (3) **the conflicts, piece by piece** — the law, Apple and Google, and the aviso's own words; the rulings it revisits and keeps; (4) **what other apps do**; (5) *trust that grows*, drawn for later; (6) what each later stage needs; (7) everything by what it costs us; (8) **five questions** — the decisions block.
**Read, with where each came from:** the live project's auth settings through Supabase's Management API, 2026-11-10 — sign-up open and **`mailer_autoconfirm` on, so a new account takes a minute and starts with no record**; the LFPDPPP of 2025, the LFPC and the federal civil code from the Chamber of Deputies' texts, Apple's guidelines (updated 2026-10-16) and its privacy-label page, Google's user-content policy, its explainer and *Data safety*, all read 2026-11-09 and cited on the page by article; Too Good To Go's, Cheaf's and OpenTable's terms, Uber's, DiDi's, Mercado Libre's and Airbnb's help pages, and four papers, the same day (some through archived copies, as the page says). **Checked:** the page's script parses (Node), and it was looked at once in headless Chrome at a desktop and a narrow width — both labs, the tables, no sideways scroll.
**Found:** (1) ⚠️ **Google counts *"user reviews"* as user content** (its explainer): terms accepted before rating, and a way to report — so even the ruled one-tap stars need a line in Itacate and a paragraph in the terms; Apple's 1.2 sits *"objectification of real people (such as 'hot-or-not' voting)"* beside it, the nearest rule to a fonda scoring named neighbours. (2) ⚠️ **A record that punishes is escaped in a minute** (`mailer_autoconfirm`), and one that rewards is not. (3) **Her record shown to other kitchens fits none of the law's exceptions** — her express authorization (LFPDPPP Art. 35; LFPC Art. 76 Bis fr. I); **a fonda refusing her runs into LFPC Art. 58**, which bars *reserva del derecho de admisión* and has no exception for no-shows; **an automatic limit is what LFPDPPP Art. 26 fr. II lets her oppose**; a first name to the kitchen she reserved at fits Art. 36 fr. IV and VII. (4) **Every app found applies no-show consequences itself, by a rule** — Cheaf, the closest to ours (pay at the store), takes away paying at the store until three card orders; OpenTable suspends after four no-shows in 12 months — and none shows a customer's record from one business to another. (5) A rating shown to the fonda at once names its rater when she hands over two plates an afternoon — the page shows them the next day, mixed.
**Decided on his behalf:** (a) ⚠️ **`10.5b-ii` split on its gate** (a split at `M` names a non-size reason — half of it waits on his answers): the discussion (`10.5b-ii-a`, done) and his picks built (`10.5b-ii-b`: the names and the ratings, their migration and both apps' additions); the row said the migration would be built in this sitting if he ruled here, else at the head of `10.5b-iii` — a row of its own keeps `10.5b-iii` an `L` and lets reserving start before he answers; (b) ⚠️ **the marker moves to `10.5b-iii`**: each of the five answers only adds to reserving's screens (a line on the sheet, a rating after *Ya lo tengo*, a name on *Apartados*), so `10.5b-iii` and `10.5b-iv` lose that gate, and **the one-tap stars of questions 7–10 move with the rest of the feedback into `10.5b-ii-b`**; (c) the page's made-up day, its people and its tags' words — proposals; (d) the five recommendations — his to rule. Each reverses with a plan edit; nothing reached a phone, the site or the database.
**Changed:** this plan (this entry; the decisions block's row; `10.5b`; `10.5b-ii` split into `10.5b-ii-a` and `10.5b-ii-b`; `10.5b-iii`, `10.5b-iv` and `10.5b-vi`); the handbook (the rows of `10.3`, `10.5b`, `10.5b-ii`, `10.5b-ii-a`, `10.5b-ii-b`, `10.5b-iii`, `10.5b-iv`, `10.5b-vi`); ADR-035 §2.7 (*What is hers*, *What she writes*); his private notes' rulings log, pushed. IT SHIPS AFTER THE MERGE — ONE UPDATE TO WERA COCINA'S TEST BUILDS AND ONE TO ITACATE'S ANDROID TEST BUILD (ITS LIST CARD TAKES THE DISTANCE IN WORDS, NOTHING A NEIGHBOUR SEES) — SO `10.5-v-d` IS THE NEXT TASK UNTIL IT SHIPS.**
**His words:** none new since the entries below.
**The estimate, written first:** `L`, at the edge of `XL` — two halves of about `M`. **The publishing half was larger than the row said:** a dish never carried a usual price (the box was drawn and sent for a surprise bag alone), so the ruling was not *"remove (opcional)"* but the box on every line, required, sent for every line and carried by *Repetir*. `publish_listing` (`0076`) already takes `usual_price` on any line and refuses one not above the price — **no migration** (read in `0072` and `0076`). The card at the foot is Itacate's list card, not yet in Wera Cocina's `src/vecinos/`. **The first-run half:** Wera's opening (`8g`) is the model; Wera Cocina had no splash hold, no opening and no cream cow; and the guard moved her to *Hoy* the instant her kitchen existed. **Not split:** neither half is gated, and both failure classes — the guard's moves, the card filling in, the opening's motion — can be seen here on the emulator against the local shop.
**What was built:**
- ***Lo que vale normalmente* on every line, required** (`@/api/listing`: `checkLine`'s `usual`, sent by `lineBody` for every line, carried by `repeatDraft`): the box under the price on a dish and a bag alike, no *(opcional)*; *Platillo 1: escribe lo que vale normalmente, para que tus vecinos vean cuánto ahorran.* when empty. **The database is unchanged** — `usual_price` stays nullable, so a build in the field still publishes without one (the field rule); `test/listing.test.ts` refuses any migration that makes it `not null`. An offer published before the rule has none, so its one-tap *Repetir* opens the form, which asks once (`planDeRepetir` runs the form's checks).
- ***Así la verán en Itacate*** between the hours and *Publicar*: **`cocina/src/vecinos/ui/TarjetaPlatillo.tsx`, Itacate's list card copied**, held code for code by `test/vecinos.test.ts`, one per line, made from what she typed (`@/lib/vistaPrevia`, pure) — her kitchen as *Ver como la ven* makes it, the form's window, each line's dish — opening nothing, measuring no distance. Until she types: the drawing's hints, *Comida corrida* (*Bolsa de pan*), *$60*, *$120* — which the price boxes now show as their placeholders too. A photo just taken is drawn from the phone (`RecienTomadas`, the photo adapter's), since it goes up only on *Publicar*.
- **The first run:** a stranger goes to **the opening** (`(auth)/hola`, `@/auth/guard`): Wera's opening in Cocina's green (`@/ui/Apertura`, a third Reanimated part, opacity and transform only) — on the splash's own frame the cow in green; the green square grows as she turns cream; a nod; the rise; then *Wera Cocina*, *Vende hasta el último plato*, ***Crear cuenta*** and ***Ya tengo cuenta*** (*Entrar*'s half). Once per phone. Wera's cream cow byte for byte, drawn green by tint (`cocina/src/marca/vaca-crema.png`); Wera's splash hold (`@/lib/splash`); the geometry read off this app's `render.sh` and `app.json` (`test/primera.test.ts`). The way in has *Volver* and, where the line was, *Tu cuenta es la misma en Wera, Wera Cocina e Itacate.* Then `10.4h`'s two screens, now ***Paso 1 de 3*** and ***Paso 2 de 3***, and — **the guard sends a kitchen made in the sign-up to `/que-ofreces`, not *Hoy*** — ***Paso 3 de 3 · ¿Qué ofreces hoy?***, her trade's drawing on her colour, *Publicar mi primera oferta* (the one form, `primera`) or *Después*; both end on **`/ya-estas`**: Itacate's page of her kitchen (*Ver como la ven*'s), titled ***Tu cocina ya está en Itacate · Así te ven tus vecinos:*** while the door would send her, ***Así te verán tus vecinos*** with *Ver como la ven*'s line otherwise, and ***Ir a Hoy***.
- **Itacate:** `@/ui/TarjetaPlatillo` takes its kitchen, offer and dish one by one and the distance in words — the list passes `distanciaTexto` — so Wera Cocina can copy it as `FilaCocina` was. Nothing a neighbour sees changes.
- **The aviso** (tu-wera.com/privacidad/cocina-e-itacate): *lo que vale normalmente si lo escribes* became *lo que vale normalmente*. The date line already reads 7 November.
**Measured:** Wera Cocina's suite **603 tests in 28 files** (569 in 26 before; `vistaPrevia` and `primera` new) — CI's tally on pull request 522; this Mac read 602 before the splash's fade was pinned — in Mexico City's zone and in UTC; Itacate's **273 in 15**, unchanged; both typechecks clean (typed routes regenerated for `/hola`, `/que-ofreces`, `/ya-estas`); the twin test seen red with Itacate's card changed alone and green again; `10.4b-iv-a-listing-contract.sh` against the local stack, **11 groups**.
**Seen on the emulator** (Android 16; Wera Cocina's debug build against the local shop; two new accounts): the opening recorded and read frame by frame — the green cow, the square with the cow cream, the nod, the rise, the words and the buttons — and on a second launch the splash held, then the opening settled; *Crear cuenta* opening *Tu cuenta* and *Ya tengo cuenta* opening *Entrar*, each with *Volver*; *Paso 1 de 3*, *Paso 2 de 3*; ***Registrar mi cocina* landing on *¿Qué ofreces hoy?***; the form with the usual price on a dish, the card whole at once with the hints, then filling in — *Enchiladas verdes*, *$55*, *Quedan 6*, then ~~$90~~ *−39%*; *Publicar* refused with the usual price empty and saying why; published, ***Tu cocina ya está en Itacate*** with today's dish on her page and *Publicaste Enchiladas verdes · Deshacer*; **the dish in the local database with `usual_price` 90.00 and `surprise` false**; Android's back from there opening *Hoy*, and back again leaving the app; signed out onto the opening, settled; a second kitchen's *Después* — ***Así te verán tus vecinos*** with *Tus vecinos verán tu página en Itacate en cuanto publiques una oferta.* — and *Ir a Hoy*; a surprise bag's card on its kitchen's own colour with *Sorpresa*.
**Found and fixed:** ⚠️ **on Android the splash fades out over 400 ms** (expo-splash-screen's default, `SplashScreenModule.kt`), over the screen behind it — recorded, the square and the nod played under the fading splash. On Android the steps now wait out the fade, so the motion starts on a clear screen; an iPhone's splash does not fade, and waits nothing (`esperaDe`, pinned to the library's source).
**Not seen:** the opening on a test build — this debug build's own splash is the old rust cow, so its hand-off showed rust turning green; the test builds' splash is the same green cow as the opening's first frame; a photo just taken drawn in the card; Itacate's list after its card's change, signed in; the iPhone. **Decided on his behalf:** (a) **the card is Itacate's own list card**, not a drawing of it, one per line; (b) **until she types, the drawing's figures** — *Comida corrida*, $60, ~~$120~~ — and the same two as hints in her price boxes; (c) the usual-price box **under the price** with its word *Lo que vale normalmente* (the drawing put *Precio de siempre* beside it); (d) an offer published before the rule: *Repetir* opens the form; (e) the opening is **Wera's, once per phone**, and on Android after the splash's fade; (f) **three steps, not the drawing's four**: `10.4h`'s screens keep the name and the trade together; (g) *Tu cuenta es la misma en Wera, Wera Cocina e Itacate.* where the line was on the way in; (h) *¿Qué ofreces hoy?* wears **her trade's drawing on her colour**; (i) **her page is Itacate's whole page**, titled *ya está* only while the door would send her — the drawing said it after *Después* too, which was not true; (j) **no *¡Listo!* screen after her first offer**: the toast with *Deshacer* over her page, as every publication since `10.5-v-c-i`; (k) Android's back on the last two screens opens *Hoy*, and nothing of the first run is kept on the phone. Each reverses with an update.

▶️ **2026-11-07 — `10.5-v-c-ii` BUILT: HER DAILY REMINDER — THE QUESTION ONCE ON *HOY*, *RECORDATORIO DIARIO* IN *MI COCINA*, A NOTIFICATION HER OWN PHONE SCHEDULES WHOSE TAP OPENS *PUBLICAR* — AND *ASÍ LA VEN* / *VER COMO LA VEN*, ITACATE'S TWO PAGES INSIDE WERA COCINA AS ITACATE'S OWN CODE, FED FROM HER ROWS. THE AVISO SAYS IT FIRST. NO MIGRATION, NO NATIVE CHANGE. IT SHIPS AFTER THE MERGE — ONE UPDATE TO WERA COCINA'S TEST BUILDS AND ONE TO ITACATE'S ANDROID TEST BUILD (ITS TWO PAGES MOVED INTO COMPONENTS) — SO `10.5-v-c-ii` IS THE NEXT TASK UNTIL IT SHIPS.**
**His words:** none new since the entries below.
**The estimate, written first:** `L`, at the edge of `XL` — two pieces of about `M`: the reminder (the rule, the question, the row and its sheet, what the phone schedules, the aviso) and the previews (about 900 lines of Itacate's parts copied and held to Itacate's files, fed from her rows). **Not split:** neither half is gated, and the reminder's failure class — whether it fires at her hour on her days, whether Android asks, whether a tap opens *Publicar* — can be seen here by moving the emulator's clock, which this session did.
**What was built** — the family-shell page as ruled (call 21, round 6's fifth zone, *Managing an offer*, *A kitchen's page*):
- **Her reminder.** `@/lib/recordatorio` (pure): her hour is the middle of the moments she published in her last two weeks — the earlier middle of an even count — rounded down to the half hour; her days are the weekdays she published on two different dates; asked after her third offer, only while the rule finds an hour and a day; the words, Expo's weekday (counted from Sunday) and a tap's rule (one of hers, within the hour; an iPhone's moment in seconds, Android's in milliseconds). `@/lib/avisos`, the one module that imports `expo-notifications` (no push token): the channel *Recordatorio diario* made before the permission is asked, one weekly notification per day of hers, every change queued in order. `@/lib/RecordatorioProvider`, in the tabs' layout: her answer for both tabs, kept on the phone per kitchen; the phone made to hold exactly her reminders on every launch and return to the front; all cancelled when the tabs leave; a tap opening *Publicar una oferta*. `@/ui/PreguntaRecordatorio` (*¿Te recordamos a las 16:00, de lunes a sábado?* · *Sueles publicar hacia las 16:10.*, *Sí, recuérdame*, *Otra hora*, ✕) under *Hoy*'s doors; *Recordatorio diario* under her trade in *Mi cocina*; `@/ui/HojaRecordatorio` — four hours around hers, the notification as it will look, *Encender recordatorio* or *Guardar* and *Apagarlo*.
- ***Así la ven* and *Ver como la ven*.** On each of today's offers in *Tus ofertas*, beside *Ya se terminó* (one dish opens its page, several her page), and beside *Editar tu página* in *Mi cocina*; under a dark bar with *Cerrar* (`@/ui/BarraVista`), no way back over the picture, nothing that opens, no distance. **`cocina/src/vecinos/` is Itacate's code** — its two pages and the parts they draw with — held to Itacate's files by `test/vecinos.test.ts`; its words are `VECINOS`. **`@/lib/comoLaVen`** makes the door's answer for her kitchen from her own rows — on offer, her point, the four-week summary — every rule held to the text of the migration that defines `on_offer_now` last (`test/comoLaVen.test.ts`), and a line under the bar while the door would not send her.
- **Itacate:** its two pages' drawing moved, unchanged, into `@/ui/PaginaDeCocina` and `@/ui/PaginaDePlatillo`, handed their data by their routes; `@/ui/FilaCocina` takes the distance in words. Nothing a neighbour sees changes.
- **Her list's read:** her last 60 publications, not 10 — four weeks at two a day, which the summary and the reminder's two weeks are worked out from.
- **The aviso** (tu-wera.com/privacidad/cocina-e-itacate, section 3 and purpose 2): the reminder, only if she asks — the hour and the days from her last two weeks, worked out on her phone, scheduled and shown by her phone, kept on it alone, off when she signs out; and *Hoy ninguna de las dos apps envía notificaciones* became *Hoy no enviamos ningún aviso desde nuestros servidores a ninguna de las dos apps*. `store/cocina-itacate.md`: no *Data safety* or Apple answer changes (nothing collected or shared).
**Measured:** Wera Cocina's suite **569 tests in 26 files** (469 in 23 before: `vecinos` 58, `comoLaVen` 16, `recordatorio` 26 new), in Mexico City's zone and in UTC; Itacate's **273 in 15**, both zones, unchanged; both typechecks clean; the twin test seen red on five breakages (a style in a whole copy, a rule in a partial one, a word, an extra imported name, a stray file) and green again; `10.4b-iv-a-listing-contract.sh` against the local stack, **11 groups**, the read sent with *60 at most*; the site's build check with placeholder secrets, fit to publish.
**Seen on the emulator** (Android 16; the two debug builds against the local shop; a kitchen with a fortnight of publications back-dated): the question on *Hoy*, exactly as above; *Otra hora*'s sheet; Android's permission prompt on *Encender*; the toast *Te recordaremos a las 16:30, de lunes a sábado*; the phone holding six weekly alarms, Monday to Saturday (`dumpsys alarm`); the reminder posted with the cow on Wera Cocina's green on the *recordatorio* channel when the clock reached its hour — **31 s and 3 min 21 s after it**, on two days; *Recordatorio diario* in *Mi cocina* and its sheet, the schedule moved to 17:00 by *Guardar*; *Así la ven* on a one-dish offer and on a two-dish one; her page's *Lo que suele ofrecer* — **the same three lines, counts and prices as the local door's own answer** (`on_offer_now`, asked as her); and Itacate after the move: the list, a dish's page, her page from its row, a dish from her page.
**Found and fixed:** the phone's clock and signal were dark on the preview's dark bar; the bar turns them light while it is up.
**Found and NOT fixed — his phone decides:** ⚠️ **a tap on the reminder opened *Publicar* 7 times of 9; twice it brought the app back where she had left it.** The 7 include the last 6 in a row and a cold start (the process killed, its alarm kept, the tap read at launch); a trace written on the phone showed JavaScript receives the tap. The two misses were early runs of a debug build attached to Metro — one coincided with that build losing Metro's connection, which a store build does not have. Not explained, so not called fixed. ⚠️ **On Android 12 and later the reminder can arrive late, up to an hour, never early:** without the exact-alarm permission — in no build, and granted only by her in Settings — Android gives it a one-hour window (`window=+1h`); on an awake phone it came within minutes. An iPhone's is on the minute. **Recommended: accept it for the pilot**; the permission is a native change, noted in `10.8`'s row for him to want or not.
**Decided on his behalf:** (a) **the previews are fed from her own rows, never by asking the door** — the door is the one read across businesses (ADR-035 §2.7) and a second reader needs a ruling; (b) the door's summary worked out again from her rows, held to the migration's text; (c) **her list's read 60 publications** (from 10); (d) *Así la ven* on an offer of several dishes opens her page; (e) the line under the bar while the door would not send her, *Tus vecinos verán tu página en Itacate en cuanto publiques una oferta.*; (f) *Cómo llegar ›* in Wera Cocina's green in the previews (its suite refuses Itacate's blue) — the one difference from Itacate; (g) the question waits until the rule finds a day, never inventing days; (h) her hour the earlier of two middles; (i) the sheet a React Native `Modal` faded in, not a spring (Reanimated stays in two parts); (j) a tap followed for an hour; (k) weekly notifications, so one can come on a day she already published; (l) the aviso's words — and the email it promises before a change of purpose not sent, on his ruling of 2026-11-06 while the only accounts are his tests (*"The only accounts are my own tests"*). Each reverses with an update.
**Changed:** Wera Cocina's `src/` (the five new modules, `src/vecinos/`, the two preview routes, *Hoy*, *Mi cocina*, *Tus ofertas*, `@/ui/OfertaDeHoy`, the tabs' layout, `@/api/listing`'s limit, the strings) and its three new tests; Itacate's two routes, `@/ui/FilaCocina` and the two new components, and two of its tests; `cocina-itacate.yml` (every migration fires it); the aviso; `store/cocina-itacate.md`; root `CLAUDE.md`, `cocina/CLAUDE.md`, `itacate/CLAUDE.md`; this plan (this entry; `10.5-v-c-ii`'s and `10.8`'s rows); the handbook (`10.5-v-c-ii`'s row).

So the evidence for the motion is a device run: a local debug build, affordable now (99 GB free; `10.5-iv-a-ii`'s session had 3 GB). One sitting, no split.
**What was built** — the family-shell page's stages 1 and 2 (his private notes' `diseno/`), as ruled on 2026-11-05:
- **The tab bar** (`(vecino)/(tabs)/`, `@/navigation/pestanas`, `@/ui/Pestanas`): *Cerca · Apartados · Mis cocinas · Tú* as data, the two middle tabs naming the rows that bring them (`10.5b`, `10.9`), so the bar draws ***Cerca*** and ***Tú***; Wera's JavaScript tabs (`expo-router/js-tabs`) with the bar drawn here — white, a line above, icon over word, the tab in use in Itacate's blue with its word heavier and a pill of the blue's tint behind its icon (never the colour alone). A kitchen's page, a dish's and *Dónde buscar* are pushed over the tabs with their *Volver*.
- ***Cerca*** — the list (`10.5-iv-a`), its head now the sheet's the map will slide under (`@/ui/Cabeza`): *Cerca de ti* in Nunito, *Según tu ubicación* or *Cerca del código postal …* with ***Cambiar*** (opens *Dónde buscar*), and ***Ordenar: Ahora*** — a button opening a sheet (`@/ui/Hoja`) with *Ahora*, *Más cerca* and *Más barato*, each with the page's line under it. Its band's *Tu cuenta* link is gone.
- ***Tú*** (`(vecino)/(tabs)/tu`): her account first, a row with nothing to tap — her name, her address (never one Apple made up) and how she signs in (*Entras con Google*, *con Apple*, *con tu correo*), from the session the phone holds; ***Dónde buscar*** with her pick; ***Aviso de privacidad***; ***Ayuda por WhatsApp*** (only on a build with his number; the chat names Itacate and her address); *Cerrar sesión* and *Eliminar mi cuenta* (`@/ui/Salidas`, unchanged); ***Itacate, de Wera*** under Wera's rust cow, and the test version's line. It replaced *Tu cuenta* (`10.5-iv-a-i`).
- ***Dónde buscar*** (`(vecino)/donde`, `@/lib/DondeBuscarProvider`) — his answer 5 of 2026-11-05 (*"We can let the user pick the codigo postal or use her location"*), which `10.5-iv-b`'s row carried: *Mi ubicación* (*Itacate la usa solo mientras la tienes abierta. Nunca sale de tu teléfono.*) or *Un código postal*, typed and placed by her phone as before (`@/ui/CodigoPostal`). ⚠️ **A code she picks now wins over her location**, until she picks *Mi ubicación* again (`@/lib/ubicacion`'s `origenDe` — it reversed `10.5-iv-a-i`'s rule that a precise location always won), and **while she searches from a code her phone is not asked at all** (`useDondeEstoy(activo)`). Her pick is React state at the root, kept nowhere, the login's own, and never read by the two pages (`test/donde.test.ts`).
- **Nunito** (`@/theme/letra`, `src/marca/letras/`): Wera's file and font byte for byte, loaded before the first screen draws, the phone's face if the load fails; every title — the band, a tab's head, a sheet's name, *Itacate aún no llega a tu zona*.
- **Itacate's blue** (`@/theme/palette`): `accion` `#24476B`, the ground of its Android icon, and its tint `#E3EAF2`, where Wera's green was — every button, link and chosen row.
- **The aviso** (tu-wera.com/privacidad/cocina-e-itacate, dated 6 November): its summary's *o el código postal que escribas*, and section 10 — a postal code also when she prefers to search from another place, in *Tú → Dónde buscar*, and Itacate not asking her location while she searches from one. The same five digits to Google from her phone, nothing to us, forgotten when the app closes — wording, not a change in what is used, so no email (its section 11).
- **The motion** (`@/theme/movimiento`, `@/ui/Llega`, `@/ui/Hoja`), the page's *Arrive* and *Sheet*: a screen's first blocks fade in and rise 8 px, 40 ms apart, once, when they mount — the way in, both tabs and *Dónde buscar* (a new order or a return to a tab moves nothing); the sheet rises with a slight spring (damping ratio 0.74) over a veil that fades in; `transform` and `opacity` only; a phone set to reduce motion shows everything at once.
**Measured:**
- **Itacate's suite 273 tests in 15 files** (was 247 in 14) — the runner's tallies, in the Mac's zone and in UTC; the typecheck clean, with the typed routes regenerated (`/tu`, `/donde` in, `/cuenta` out).
- **17 broken copies, each red on the test aimed at it**, every file restored byte for byte (its hash read back): Wera's green back; a `width` animated in the entrance; the entrance without reduce-motion; native tabs; the pill taken away; her way out above her account on *Tú*; the help message without the app's name; Apple's made-up address shown; her location first again over a code she picked; *Dónde buscar* importing the location module; a kitchen's page reading her pick; the phone still asked while she searches from a code; the pick kept for the next login; the font loaded with no fallback; a byte changed in the copy of Nunito, and in the cow; a screen in the tabs' folder that is not a tab in use.

An update only: the column stays nullable, so a build in the field still publishes without it (the field rule), and `publish_listing` already refuses a usual price not above the price. **The argument against:** one more box while she is busy, and a fonda can no longer publish at her usual price — every offer must be cheaper than usual. The other way is to keep it optional and let the line be a promise most offers keep |
>
> | **Until when may a shop's offer run: today only, or tomorrow too?** (his question of 2026-11-06 on the family-shell page, https://claude.ai/artifact/5bztU1ZEgXWpnjwPkCzcZf, call 42: *"How about if the tienda owner wants to allow the restaurant to pass for the food the day after? Drawn and playable on the page (version 10, stage T). ⚠️ **Cheap now:** inside `10.6a`'s migration — a shop's window may run to the end of tomorrow, Mexico City time; a kitchen's keeps its twelve hours. Several days and a shop's own hours wait until shops ask. **The argument against:** overnight, the reserved products sit on her shelf, where the counter can sell them by mistake (the encargado sets them aside, his answer G, and *Inicio* lists them); and a jitomate *muy maduro* tonight is a day riper by noon tomorrow. The other way is today only, as drawn before |

▶️ **2026-11-06 — WERA COCINA'S *HOY* AS A HUB, HER OFFERS IN A ROOM OF THEIR OWN (THE FAMILY-SHELL PAGE, ROUND 6): HIS DIRECTIONS WRITTEN INTO `10.5-v-c`, THE ARRANGEMENT DRAWN FOR HIS LOOK. NO CODE CHANGED. `10.5-v-b` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words** (the afternoon of 6 November in Mexico City, looking at the page): *"I would like us to improve now the view from Cocina's side. Looks like a lot of scrolling just to visualize what are the cocinas ofering. Perhaps we can make it a module of its own. The "Así te ven los vecinos" section in Hoy is too much, the owner can see this in the Mi Cocina section. Also the user should be able to dismiss the "Tu página en Itacate" section in hoy screen. Arrange that screen to show the users everything in a dashborad/hub way, for them to easily reach the main actions with some indicators such as remaining items. The buttons to set up reminders are good as well as the one to publish a new oferta. Give it a good thought on what and how to display and which are the actions that the user should be able to do or reach from this screen. The rest of the things should be intuitevly and easily (without a lot of scrolling) reachable from the other tabs."*
**Ruled (his directions), in `10.5-v-c`'s row:** *Hoy* a hub with indicators; her offers in a room of their own, *Tus ofertas*; *Así te ven los vecinos* only in *Mi cocina*; *Tu página en Itacate* closable on *Hoy*; *Publicar una oferta* and the reminder's buttons kept.

Driven at every stage in headless Chrome, no script error.

**What he was told first, read from the code:** the zone is one setting in Wera Cocina (`cocina/src/lib/google.ts`'s `ZONA_DE_BUSQUEDA`, a rectangle) that Itacate will copy; nothing in the database names a region (the door takes no area), and neither the aviso, the terms nor the store listings name the zone. **Ruled:** Itacate's region is Wera Cocina's search zone — a neighbour inside it finds every kitchen on offer, and outside it reads *Itacate aún no llega a tu zona*; widening it later is that setting in each app, sent over the air.

▶️ **2026-11-04, night — HIS RULING: EACH KITCHEN IS DRAWN AT GOOGLE'S POINT FOR HER ADDRESS, KEPT AS A 30-DAY COPY — ITS ID KEPT FOR GOOD, THE POINT REFRESHED BY HER OWN PHONE AND CLEARED EVERY NIGHT — AND USED ONLY TO DRAW, NEVER TO FILTER; FOR THE PILOT THE DOOR SENDS EVERY KITCHEN ON OFFER, SO NO POSTAL-CODE TABLE. THE DECISIONS BLOCK LOSES ITS POSTAL-CODE ROW, AND `10.5-i-b` IS THE NEXT TASK. AND A PRICE SHEET OF EVERY PROVIDER, IN HIS PRIVATE NOTES.**
**His words:** *"I need the Itacate user to be able to see the pins in the map while navigating."*; *"If we store the coordinates from the original address sugestion, what are the consecuences, what's the workaround in terms of refreshing our database each month or so? What are the risks?"*; *"Why can't we run a filter on Google's data?"*; *"What does refreshed from her phone implies? Does the refresh run in the background?"*; then *"Yes, record it and follow your recommendation"* — and a record of what each provider commits us to, *"for us to consult"*, wherever it belongs.
**Read that night, Google's terms as published** (Service Specific Terms, last modified 2026-10-28; Terms of Service, 2026-08-26): Places coordinates may be cached up to 30 consecutive days, then deleted (§14.3), Geocoding's the same (§6.3.1 — §6.3.2's longer cache is one person's alone), place IDs for good (§3); Places and Geocoding content never with a non-Google map (§14.2, §6.2; ToS 3.2.3(e)); and *"use latitude/longitude values from the Places API as an input for point-in-polygon analysis"* is named as forbidden (ToS 3.2.3(c)(iv), *No Creating Content From Google Maps Content*). ⚠️ **This corrects the session's first answer the same night**, which had said Google's point may never be stored and shown to other people: true only past 30 days. Google's own *Autocomplete for end user addresses* exception covers the street address she picks, not its coordinates. The prices read are in his private notes, never here.
**Ruled:** (1) **Google's point for her address, kept as a 30-day copy**: the place's ID for good, the point with the day it was fetched, written when she registers or changes her address — the suggestion she picks already carries it — and refreshed by Wera Cocina when it opens and the point is over 7 days old, while she uses the app and with nothing on screen; a scheduled job in the database clears any point older than 30 days every night; her own pin, when she sets one, wins. (2) **Drawn only**: Itacate draws each kitchen at its point, on Google's map on both phones; nothing filters, sorts or counts on Google's points on the server — a check in CI refuses it. (3) **For the pilot the door takes no area**: every kitchen on offer in the zone reaches her phone, which draws them as she moves the map and sorts the list nearest first, with the distance shown — her location never leaves her phone; a typed postal code is asked of Google by her own phone, for her alone. (4) **No postal-code table for the pilot**: when the zone outgrows one answer, an area filter comes back on our own data — her own pin, or a table of postal-code centres (`10.5-0`'s measure) — added without breaking a build. (5) **The aviso** says Google's point is kept up to 30 days and refreshed while she uses the app, and the email it promises goes first (his test accounts only, today).
**Decided on his behalf:** the refresh age, 7 days; the clean-up as `pg_cron` inside the database — no server key and no server calling Google — the project's first scheduled job, with ADR-035 amended; the hard 1.5 and 3 km cutoffs on Google's points held until Google's support answers (the listings row's written question now asks about the 30-day copy and the distance too), nearest first meanwhile; `10.5-i-b` resized from the table to this, lanes Backend and Cocina, still `L`. **Not recommended, and said:** a server refreshing every point — a Google key not locked to our apps, and kitchens refreshed that nobody sees.
**Changed:** the decisions block (the postal-code row gone; the region row's typed postal code; the listings row's question); `10.5-i-b` rewritten and the next task; `10.5-iv`; `5P-e`'s marker given back; the progress line; ADR-035 (§2.2's *Address search* row, §2.7's one door, and a revision); the handbook; the root `CLAUDE.md` (a paid provider's change updates his private price sheet); his private notes — the ruling in `negocio/decisiones.md`, and in `negocio/costos.md` a section for each provider: what in the ecosystem uses it, its free amount and its price beyond, read that night, with the source; and the 2026-10-10 working day's four entries moved, unedited, to `docs/plan/archive/status-log-2026-10-10.md`, so `## Position` keeps room under its 1,400 lines.
**Waiting on him:** nothing new — Itacate's region and Google's listings clause (before `10.5-iv`), questions 7–10 (before `10.5b`).

**Decided on his behalf:** (a) ⚠️ **the photos are read by anyone signed in only while the kitchen exists and is active** — narrower than the ruling's words, to keep for a kitchen what Wera's aviso promises of a deleted shop's photos (out of everyone's reach at once, deleted by hand within 30 days); `kitchen_folder_ok` answers that one boolean past `workspace`'s policy. Reversing it: one policy. (b) **The folder must be a kitchen's, to write as to read** — a shop's owner is an owner too, and `photo_folder_ok` alone would let her put files there nobody could read. (c) **Pictures only, 2 MB a file** (JPEG, PNG or WebP): the first bucket nearly anyone may write (anyone can open a kitchen) and everyone signed in reads — never a page or an SVG served from the project's address; widening is free. (d) ⚠️ **Her link must open Google Maps** — https and `maps.app.goo.gl`, `goo.gl/maps`, `g.page`, `google.com/maps` or `maps.google.com`, with `.mx` or without, no dot segments, at most 1,000 characters — because Itacate shows it as *Ver en Google Maps*; it is what a fonda can paste in `10.5-ii`, and a host added later refuses no build. (e) **`set_kitchen_page` takes one object of the fields to change**, so a section added to every page later is never erased by an older build that does not know it. (f) ⚠️ **The names — cheap now, a new migration once a build uses them:** `kitchen_page` (`sentence`, `color`, `cover_path`, `logo_path`, `maps_link`), `listing_line.image_path` (`0063`'s name, not the row's `photo`), the bucket `cocina`, the codes `chile`, `mole`, `nopal`, `talavera`, `cempasuchil`, `jamaica`, each NULL its slot's default. (g) The account line's words. (h) A shop's refusal is `0072`'s `TD013`, and the sentence one line, as `directions` is. (i) The Storage check makes its people through the auth server's admin door and signs their tokens with the local secret: six sign-ups a run, nine runs in its falsifier and the job's other checks would pass the auth server's limit. Every one reverses with an additive migration or an edit; nothing is seeded.

✅ **2026-11-03, night — `10.5-0` DONE: THE POSTAL-CODE CENTRES MEASURED ACROSS THE SEARCH'S ZONE — INEGI'S BUSINESSES PLACE A CODE'S POINT WITHIN 1 KM OF 90% OF MEXICO CITY'S RESTAURANTS AND 77% OF THE STATE OF MEXICO'S, GEONAMES' OF 56% AND 34%. THE PAGE FOR HIS RULING RECOMMENDS INEGI'S POINTS, GEONAMES' ONLY FOR A CODE WITH FEWER THAN FIVE BUSINESSES, AND NEVER THE POSTAL SERVICE'S CATALOGUE, WHICH MAY NOT BE PASSED ON. NOTHING BUILT. `10.5-i` SPLIT ON THAT RULING: `10.5-i-a` (KITCHENS' PAGES AND DISH PHOTOS) IS THE NEXT TASK; `10.5-i-b` (THE DOOR AND THE TABLE) WAITS ON HIS ANSWER.**
**His words:** none new — the row had no gate; his rulings of 2026-11-03 (address first, then the three ways in) are why the table is asked.
**The estimate, written first:** `S`, as the row said — measured `M`: the State of Mexico's INEGI file, the postal service's catalogue and Mexico City's postal-code shapes joined the measurement, and a research agent read each source's licence at its source. Read before measuring (the plan, his private notes, the sources — not a device): (1) the twenty addresses can be drawn again — `10.4c`'s sampler and seed over the cached `comedores-cdmx` file (two copies on this Mac, one hash) give the same twenty in the same order, twelve of their recorded points trusted; (2) INEGI publishes no postal-code layer (its vector layers stop at the block): its own data with a postal code and a point is the business directory, DENUE — Mexico City's May 2026 file on this Mac since 2026-10-21; (3) GeoNames' readme says what its points are — a settlement name looked up in its gazetteer, else *"an average lat/lng of 'neighbouring' postal codes"* — not centres; (4) a sharper test than twelve doors: every restaurant DENUE lists, each scored against an INEGI point computed without restaurants; (5) the search's box (`ZONA_DE_BUSQUEDA`) also takes in codes of eight other states; (6) no one-way door: nothing built, no migration, nothing seeded — the table is `10.5-i`'s, his to rule.
**Measured** (scripts on this Mac; each door's great-circle distance from its source's point for its code; a door with no point counts as infinitely far):
- **55,752 restaurants in Mexico City and 89,406 in the State of Mexico** (DENUE 05/2026, SCIAN 7225, out of 1,283,458 businesses with a code and a point). Within 1 km: INEGI 90.3% and 76.9%, GeoNames 55.7% and 33.6%; within 3 km: INEGI 98.8% and 96.3%, GeoNames 83.9% and 72.7%; median 369 m and 525 m against 825 m and 1.6 km; no point at all: INEGI 32 and 19, GeoNames 700 and 1,624. *Comida corrida y a la carta* (7,781 and 6,079), within 1 km: INEGI 89.4% and 77.2%, GeoNames 55.3% and 33.6%. GeoNames at its best case — each door against the nearest of its code's rows — 62.8% within 1 km in the city: the gap is not how its rows were averaged.
- **Where GeoNames misses:** Xochimilco's median 3.4 km, Milpa Alta's and Tláhuac's 2.1 km (INEGI's 273 to 350 m there); Toluca's 3.0 km and Ixtapaluca's 3.2 km; 598 of its 1,395 Mexico City codes share their point with another; Tláhuac's 13600 and 13640 placed in Letrán Valle, Benito Juárez, 26 km from Mixquic. INEGI's 90th percentile stays between 723 m and 1.1 km in every alcaldía.
- **The twelve trusted test addresses:** INEGI places eleven, all within 588 m; GeoNames the same eleven, closer on three, and misses three by 1.6 to 6.9 km. The twelfth carries 13620, on no list and used by no business — a typing error in the file; INEGI's 13600 is 179 m from its door.
- **Against the postal service's own list** (its catalogue, exported for both states): 1,110 codes in Mexico City and 2,285 in the State of Mexico; GeoNames places 1,107 and 2,250; INEGI has five or more businesses in 1,043 and 1,344, and only 119 of the 145,158 restaurants sit in a code with fewer. **Codes in use off the list:** the postal service split old codes into barrio codes (Santa Ana Tlacotenco's 12900, 401 businesses, is 12910 to 12950 on the list), and 1,430 codes in use are on no list, 211 with twenty or more businesses — INEGI places them, GeoNames has 40 of the 211.
- **Mexico City's postal-code shapes** (its open-data portal; 1,215, Correos de México's of 2021): the middle of each shape is within 1 km of 87.5% of the city's restaurants, with no shape for 983 of them; 46,323 of the 54,769 restaurants whose code has a shape sit inside it.

**Licences, read at their source:** INEGI's *Términos de Libre Uso* allow commercial use and products built on its data, with *"Fuente: INEGI, …"*, a line saying what we did to it, and nothing suggesting INEGI made or endorses the result; GeoNames' readme, CC BY 4.0 with credit; **the postal service's catalogue**, its first line — *"se proporciona en forma gratuita para uso particular, no estando permitida su comercialización, total o parcial, ni su distribución a terceros bajo ningún concepto"* — so counting only; Mexico City's portal under the city's open licence, the shapes Correos de México's. The postal service's own 2026 shapes sit on datos.gob.mx under CC BY 4.0 (updated 2026-10-16), but its server refused every download from this Mac (403).
**What shipped:** the page for his ruling, https://claude.ai/artifact/WxSEFxamNsQUYW9HTW3HqR — the recommendation and the argument against, both states' curves, where GeoNames misses, the twelve, the coverage, the licences, what it means for the door; the method and the scripts in his private notes (`ubicacion/research/2026-11-03-centros-de-codigo-postal.md`), the full addresses not kept; the decisions block's postal-code row carries the measured recommendation.
**Decided on his behalf:** (a) the points' definitions — GeoNames' rows averaged, INEGI's median latitude and longitude; (b) restaurants as the doors, scored out of sample, and as the stand-in for where neighbours are; (c) the recommendation's floor of five businesses, with GeoNames below it; (d) ⚠️ **`10.5-i` split on its gate** (the working agreement's reason 1): his answer blocks the door and its table, not a kitchen's page, a dish's photo or their bucket — so `10.5-i-a` goes next and `10.5-i-b` waits; two additive migrations where one was planned; (e) the region row's brief: the postal codes she picks from come from the table, never the catalogue. Every one reverses with a document edit; none touches the database or a build.
**Found on the way:** (1) ⚠️ **INEGI's State of Mexico file is not behind a CAPTCHA**, as `10.4c`'s measurement note said: the single file's link answers *"Esta liga ya no existe"* because the state is published in two parts, which download with a plain request — the CAPTCHA guards INEGI's pages, not its files; (2) the region row recommended *"her postal code picked from the postal service's own list, carried in the app"*, which the catalogue's licence forbids — fixed in its brief; (3) GeoNames lacks codes in daily use and keeps 288 Mexico City codes the current list no longer has.
**Changed:** the decisions block's postal-code row (its brief; it blocks `10.5-i-b`) and the region row's brief; `10.5-0` done; `10.5-i` split, `10.5-i-a` new and next, `10.5-i-b` new; `10.5-ii`'s, `10.5-iii`'s and `10.5-iv`'s gates; the handbook (the `10.3`, `10.5` and `10.5-0` rows, rows for `10.5-i-a` and `10.5-i-b`); his private notes (the measurement and its scripts, and `negocio/decisiones.md`); and the 2026-10-02 working day's three status-log entries moved, unedited, to `docs/plan/archive/status-log-2026-10-02.md` — `## Position` stood at 1,394 of its 1,400 lines with this entry, and the next would have gone over. Nothing merged into the apps, nothing deployed, no update.
**Waiting on him:** his answer on the page — `10.5-i-b` waits on it; and, before Wera Cocina's version 5 reaches his testers, what the `10.4g-ii` entry below lists.

▶️ **2026-11-03, evening — HIS WORD: THE SEARCH WORKS ON HIS IPHONE (`10.4g-i`'s look). HIS RULING: ADDRESS FIRST — HER ADDRESS ON A SCREEN OF ITS OWN, THE PIN A SUGGESTION SHE NEVER HAS TO TOUCH, NOTHING OF GOOGLE'S KEPT. `10.4h` (NEW, AHEAD OF `10.4g-ii`) IS THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words:** *"The search works amazing but when the screen moves to the pin in the map it isn't allowing me to confirm the location without moving the map/pin."*; *"the overall experience i want the user to have is that of Uber Eats when setting his delivery address"*; *"Why can't we use the address itself? We will be using it for the user to open Google Maps and ask for instructions either way no?"*; *"Split the address set up in a different screen to the one where we are determining the Tipo de Cocina … once the user finds the address, the pin seems set in the map in small (the user can change it if he clicks on the map) but he doesn't have to tap the map at all … The address is the most relevant thing both for business owners and for the Itacate users … The pin is only indicative."*; then *"Let's go with your proposal an your recommendation"*, and *"Yes"* to the small migration it needs.
**Answered first, read that day:** the pin waits for her own move because Google's coordinates may be kept 30 days (Places, Service Specific Terms §14.3) or for the one person who asked and never across people (Geocoding, §6.3.2; the terms last modified 2026-10-28); the Places policy's *Autocomplete for end user addresses* covers the address she picks and nothing more (updated 2026-11-02); and a pin nudged off Google's point is content made from Google's (ToS §3.2.3(c), 2026-08-26), so it was not proposed. The first proposal (one touch on her door): https://claude.ai/artifact/8xzBk3qqtr3buRJ9Bwh1xo, replaced by the one he chose: https://claude.ai/artifact/45eNGq6YLmMrV3DF6Lfa1D.
**Ruled:** **address first.** Registering in two screens — her kitchen (name and trade), then *¿Dónde está tu cocina?* with the search, her five boxes, a small map with the pin where Google places her address (looked up live, never kept), her line of directions and *Registrar mi cocina* — and no touch on the map needed. The server's *what is near me* (`10.5-i`'s door) uses her own point, or her postal code's centre from open data (the decisions block: a seed, his yes at that task). ⚠️ **His note "the pin is only indicative" replaces the precision of his ruling 4 of 2026-10-28** (*"the highest possible precision"*): the address is the source; a kitchen that wants her exact door sets it. **And one small additive migration in `10.4h`, his "Yes"**: her directions saved without a pin, and her own pin cleared.

**His words:** after trying slice 1 on his iPhone, *"The pin works fine. The search bar still hurts a bit when trying to find addresses. The pin sits at the middle fine but sometimes it is not defining an address properly after draging it somewhere. Can we do anything else to enhance the experience? … the closest to what Uber Eats or Rappi offers"*; then *"So, the only reason why we don't do full Google Maps is due to costs? … Could we implement it for free for now? … I'd like to move on, with something fairly cheap that delivers an amazing and profesial experience"*; then *"this allows us to show the restaurants close to you with no additional cost either in the map or with a list?"*; then ***"go, send me the Google Cloud steps"***.
**Answered first:** the card under the pin is the phone's own maps' guess for the point (`addressForCoordinate`) — a neighbour's number, another street, or nothing when Apple limits the app — and never what is saved; Uber Eats, Rappi and DiDi keep the address and the pin apart. **Apple's own as-you-type search, measured that night** on the twenty addresses (`MKLocalSearchCompleter`, the four moments of her typing, with and without her colonia, and with a 2 km head start around the map's opening point; each first suggestion resolved with `MKLocalSearch`): with the whole number, on her block for 5 to 8 of 20 — against 11 for the geocoder the iPhone already asks. No better. Cost was never what kept Google off the iPhone (the owner's private notes hold the costing): Google's terms forbid its search results on another company's map (ToS 3.2.3(e)), and Google's map on the iPhone is a new build. Showing the kitchens near a neighbour costs nothing from Google, on the map or in a list — the map is free on phones, and the kitchens are our own data.
**Ruled:** `10.4g` — Wera Cocina on Google on both phones: Google's map on the iPhone too, and on her kitchen's small map; Google's suggestions as she types, in the form's street box and in the map's box, and picking one fills her five boxes (Google's *Autocomplete for end user addresses*: the address she picks may be kept when it is her own); the map opens on Google's point; the card under the pin names her address — what is saved — and warns only when Google names another street there; LocationIQ and the phone's own search come out; the aviso's section 3 says Google on both phones, emailed to every account before it applies; a terms page carries Google's notice. **Measured first:** if Google's first suggestion is not on her block more often than the iPhone's 11 of 20, the session stops and tells him.
**His word on slice 1:** *"The pin works fine"* — the pin half of `10.5`'s gate; the search half waits for `10.4g-i` on his iPhone.
**His hands, sent in the conversation** (the handbook's *Google's search for Wera Cocina*): in the Google Cloud project of Android's map key, Places API (New), Geocoding API and Maps SDK for iOS switched on; the Android key allowed the first two; an iPhone key locked to `mx.wera.cocina` and `mx.wera.vecino` and to the three; a budget alert and daily limits; `GOOGLE_MAPS_IOS_API_KEY` among Expo's organization variables (*Sensitive*, `preview` and `production`).
**Estimated before taking it:** `XL`, about two sittings — the reasons are `10.4g`'s row. And `10.5`, estimated the same session before his ruling: `XL`, about five sittings, not three or four — split in four (`10.5-i` to `10.5-iv`), the three questions it found parked in the decisions block with recommendations.

✅ **2026-11-02, late night — `10.4f` DONE: WERA COCINA'S SEARCH COVERS MEXICO CITY AND THE STATE OF MEXICO, AND ITS QUESTION CARRIES HER CITY — MEASURED FIRST: WIDENING THE ZONE ALONE FOUND ALMOST NOTHING IN THE STATE'S TOWNS, THE CITY MADE IT WORK, AND MEXICO CITY LOST NOTHING. ALL JAVASCRIPT, OVER THE AIR. `10.5` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK, ONCE HE SAYS SLICE 1 WORKS.**
**His words:** the entry below.
**The estimate, written first:** the row is `S` — one value and a sentence — but the zone is what kept answers from other states (`10.4c`: unbounded, a Milpa Alta street's first suggestion came from 344 km away), so a wider one had to be measured on Mexico City first; measured `M`, one sitting. Read before building: the zone is one value, `VALLE_DE_MEXICO` (`-99.40,19.05,-98.85,19.75`), which LocationIQ is bounded by and the phone's box is read out of (`CAJA_DEL_VALLE`), so both providers follow it; nothing native — the module takes the box from JavaScript (its two comments still name the Valle de México: left as they are, because any change under `modules/` moves the runtime and no update would then reach the builds); the map opens on Mexico City when the opening search finds nothing, which is unchanged.
**The zone:** INEGI's extreme coordinates of the State of Mexico (*Aspectos Geográficos, Estado de México, 2021*, cuadro 1.1: N 20°17'09", S 18°22'01", E 98°35'49", W 100°36'47"), a hundredth of a degree wider each way — `-100.62,18.36,-98.59,20.29`. Mexico City lies inside it whole. A box: it also takes in the edges of the states around (Pachuca, Cuernavaca, Taxco); Guadalajara, Monterrey, Puebla, Querétaro and Tlaxcala stay outside.
**First, measured** (this Mac and the emulator, 2026-11-02, late night; every number from the scripts' output): the twenty Mexico City addresses of `10.4c` and **eleven of the State of Mexico's municipal seats outside the old zone** (INEGI's cuadro 1.2, drawn with a fixed seed from the 88 outside it; each one's street address from Apple's reverse lookup of the seat — so Apple's own answers there prove only that the zone lets them through), each asked as the app asks — what she types with her colonia; the same with her city; the opening question — under the old zone and the new: LocationIQ through the app's own functions, Google's geocoder from a debug build on the emulator through the module, Apple's on this Mac with the module's call. **The list's first row within 300 m:** Mexico City's twenty — on Android 14 of 20 in every case, on an iPhone 12 → 12 → 14 (old zone; new zone; new zone with her city); **the State of Mexico's eleven — on Android 0 (old zone) → 1 (new zone) → 7 (new zone with her city)**, LocationIQ's alone 0 → 1 → 8. Most of the seats' addresses have no colonia, so a street and number alone matched the wrong town; with her city, the right one. While she types the street, LocationIQ with her city did no worse (the whole street: 5 → 6 of 20 on her block).
**What shipped:** `@/lib/buscar` — `ZONA_DE_BUSQUEDA` (was `VALLE_DE_MEXICO`) and `CAJA_DE_BUSQUEDA` (was `CAJA_DEL_VALLE`), `preguntaConSuDireccion` (was `preguntaConColonia`: what she typed and, with no comma, her colonia and her city or alcaldía), which `preguntaConPuerta`, `useSugerencias`, `usePuertas` and *Buscar* ask through; the not-found sentence — *No encontramos esa calle en la Ciudad de México ni en el Estado de México. Escríbela de otra forma, o busca tu cocina en el mapa.*
**Measured:** Wera Cocina's typecheck clean; **Vitest 303 in 17 files** (the runner's report; 297 before), green under `TZ=UTC` too — the zone held to INEGI's extremes of the state and of Mexico City, five of the state's towns in it and five other states' cities out of it, Toluca's answer offered; **three broken copies, each red** — the zone back to the Valle de México, the box asking without her city, the sentence without the area — each file restored byte for byte; Itacate 6 in 2; `wera-runtime.sh`: Wera's runtime unchanged (iOS `d308ba9f`, Android `5a4a7a5d`); **Wera Cocina's unchanged — iOS `f4801353`, Android `4df70bf3`**, so it reaches the `10.4d-ii` builds over the air; `5R-d-ii-site-build.sh` fit to publish.
**Decided on his behalf:** (a) **a box, not the state's outline** — it takes in the edges of Hidalgo, Morelos, Querétaro, Michoacán and Guerrero beside the State of Mexico; drawing the outline buys nothing a fonda would notice; (b) **her city in the box's question** — measured, the change that makes the State of Mexico work; invisible to her; (c) **the not-found sentence names the two places** — the one a fonda in another state reads; (d) the aviso's section 3 says the city goes with what she types — the same address she gave, already sent as the map opens, to the same three companies — so, as in `10.4e`, no new email beyond the one already owed. **First, measured** (scratch scripts on this Mac; every number from their output): twenty addresses drawn with a fixed seed from Mexico City's open-data list of its *comedores comunitarios* — small kitchens on ordinary streets in all sixteen alcaldías; the 263 of 483 with a plain street number — asked of LocationIQ as the app asks, and of Apple on this Mac (`MKLocalSearchCompleter`, the top three resolved by `MKLocalSearch`; `CLGeocoder` for the five boxes). ⚠️ **The file's own points are uneven**: Apple's reverse lookup put 12 on the stated street, 6 in another colonia or alcaldía, 2 uncertain — so both counts are given. **On her block (≤ 100 m):** LocationIQ's first suggestion 6 of 20, its best of five 7 (5 and 5 of the 12 credible); the five boxes searched, 3 to 5 by the query's form; Apple's first suggestion 8 (8 of 12) — and nothing at all for 7 of the 20 — and Apple's geocoder on the five boxes 11 (9 of 12). **Within 300 m:** LocationIQ's first, 10 of 20 (8 of 12); Apple's geocoder 14 (12 of 12). **LocationIQ carried a house number in 4 of its 47 answers; Apple's geocoder in 20 of 20.** Without the Valle de México bound, the first suggestion for a Milpa Alta street was in another state; **without the colonia, a street and number found the right street for 4 of 20, with it 12** (best of five, within 300 m). So, by the ruling's own test, **LocationIQ misses too often**: the phone's own search for the release build is in the decisions block, his ruling then, as the row said — and the over-the-air half was built as ruled, because it already brings most fondas to their street instead of the whole city.

▶️ **2026-10-31, night — HIS RULING: WERA COCINA'S LOCATION STEP IS REDONE AS THE BIG APPS DO IT, WITHIN EVERY PROVIDER'S TERMS — A FLAT STREET MAP, AN ADDRESS SEARCH WITH SUGGESTIONS THAT ONLY MOVES THE MAP, HER OWN TAP AS THE PIN. `10.4c` IS THE NEXT TASK, ONCE HIS FREE LOCATIONIQ ACCOUNT EXISTS.**
**His words:** *"Let's go with your recommendation. put it next to clear context and do it."* — to the session's assessment of 2026-10-31, which he asked for after trying Wera Cocina's first iPhone build (*"I'm concerned the pin setting isn't good. The person has to navigate in the map to pin her location. Shouldn't it work with a address search?"*, then *"Assess if this is the closest we can get to a 'pristine' experience while still being in compliance … the closest we can get to currently existing experiences with Uber Eats or Rappi … InDrive … check if we can show the more flat default view instead"*). The evidence, with its sources, is in his private notes; only what the code needs is here.
**What it found, read 2026-10-31 in each party's own pages:** **Apple's licence (Developer Program License Agreement, Attachment 6) forbids storing its map data — coordinates included — beyond a temporary, limited basis, using it in a derived database, showing it publicly, or showing it on a non-Apple map; Google's terms keep Places coordinates 30 days and never on a non-Google map, and keep Geocoding results to the one end user who asked.** So a provider's point may only move the map: **the pin kept is her own tap**, and the address kept is the one she typed. **OpenStreetMap-based providers let results be kept; LocationIQ publishes a processing agreement** — an *encargado*, so a *remisión* under the LFPDPPP, where sending her typed address to Google would be a *transferencia*. ⚠️ **Play asks a declaration for precise location from November 2026, and from 27 January 2027 Android 17's own location button for one-time requests, address pickers named** — so no *Usar mi ubicación* now. The delivery apps use a standard street map, never satellite; react-native-maps 1.27.2 draws one (`standard`, `mutedStandard` on an iPhone) with a one-word change. A tap must stay, beside any drag (WCAG 2.2, SC 2.5.7).
**Found, for later rows:** ⚠️ **Google's ToS 3.2.3(d)(iii) — no use of its Core Services "in a listings or directory service or to create or augment an advertising product"** — to be read before Itacate's map of kitchens (`10.5`) and before any promotion on a map; and whether *Data safety*'s *collected, not shared* holds for the Maps SDK's own data, Google acting as a controller by its terms.
**Changed:** `10.4c` (new) carries the ruling and the next-task marker, moved from `8i`; `10.5`'s row reads 3.2.3(d)(iii) first; the handbook's rows and a section for his account. Nothing built, merged into the apps, or deployed.
**His account, the same night:** made, and its access token given in the conversation — stored as **`EXPO_PUBLIC_LOCATIONIQ_TOKEN`** in Cocina's EAS project (`preview` and `production`, *Sensitive*, read back equal in both) and in the gitignored `cocina/.env.local`; no code reads it yet, so no runtime and no update moves. One autocomplete request answered 200: for a Roma Norte street and number, three suggestions, street-level, no house number — counting how often that happens is `10.4c`'s first step.

**His words:** *"Follow your recommendation"* — to `10.4b-ii`'s closing message, which recommended making the four settings `10.4b-iii`'s row lists now, so that row can end in its update without waiting on him.
**Set, and read back:** **the hosted Apple provider's client ids are `mx.wera.app,mx.wera.cocina`, and the redirect list is `tienda://**,mx.bserafin.wera://**,mx.wera.app://**,mx.wera.cocina://**`** — his command, a `PATCH` of those two fields to the Management API's `config/auth` (`HTTP 200`), because a session's own is refused by the permission check (*Modify Shared Resources*); read back with a `GET` the same morning: both as sent, and Apple, Google and email sign-in on, email confirmation off, phone sign-in off and the built-in sender as before. **Cocina's EAS project (`@wera-app/cocina`) holds `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`** — the project's own variables, `preview` and `production`, with Wera's values, set by a session the night of 29 October; `eas env:list` read 2026-10-30 lists both beside the map key and `google-services.json`, and Wera's project is unchanged. **Cocina's runtime does not move with them**: computed as the update runbook computes it, with and without the two values, one hash per platform (iOS `df478d4f…`, Android `ec232a2d…`) — `app.config.ts` reads only the map key, and Metro writes an `EXPO_PUBLIC_` value into the JavaScript bundle, not the native build.
**The fourth, his, at midday — his word: *"Done, grouped Cocina under Wera in Apple"*.** **Cocina grouped under Wera's App ID** — developer.apple.com/account → *Certificates, IDs & Profiles* → *Identifiers* → `mx.wera.cocina` → *Sign In with Apple* → *Edit* → *Group with an existing primary App ID* → `mx.wera.app` → *Save*. What it found that the row does not say: (1) **an app's calls cannot be read from its source**: the outbox builds its argument names at run time (`p_` + the payload's keys, `@/api/flush`) from conditional spreads in the cart, and three readers take the table as a parameter; (2) **which build each phone runs is written down nowhere** — the pilot Android was last recorded as owing the 2026-10-21 build, and a Play tester can sit on an old version code — so *the oldest build in use* could only be guessed (`eas build:list` holds 18 Wera builds on ten runtimes, back to 2026-10-10); (3) a phone runs its build's own bundle or any update published to its runtime, so the code in use is every commit since the oldest build, not one; (4) Cocina and Itacate call nothing yet. **So the check holds a superset that needs neither**: everything a signed-in phone can reach.

A removal passes only when its migration declares it — `-- field-rule: breaks <kind> <key> — <why no build in use calls it>`. It also refuses an applied migration edited and a new one numbered at or below the newest, which nothing checked before. `db.yml` gains its tenth job, *every call a build could make still answers*, with the falsifier.
