▶️ **2026-10-27, night — `10.1b`: THE HOSTED DATABASE KEEPS THE WALLS — `0071` TAKES BACK WHAT ITS DEFAULTS GRANTED, AND ONE QUERY READS THE WALLS IN CI AND IN THE SHOP. `10.2` IS THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words:** none new — the walls were ruled (*nothing readable without an account*, 2026-10-27; `auth_full_name` *granted to nobody*, 2026-09-18), and `10.1` made this row and put it first.
**The estimate, written first:** `M`, as the row says. What it found that the row does not say: (1) **Supabase applies its new defaults to every existing project on 2026-10-30** (its changelog 45329) — tables and sequences only, and existing objects keep their grants, so the platform would have closed nothing that was open; (2) the hosted project carries a function no migration made, `rls_auto_enable()` — Supabase's event trigger that turns RLS on for a new table; (3) ⚠️ **`purging_workspace` must stay granted to a signed-in person**: three trigger functions call it as the writer, and PostgreSQL checks EXECUTE when it prepares an expression, before `tg_op = 'DELETE' and …` can short-circuit — so a manager's plain product or supplier edit needs it; (4) `adjust_stock_delta` has no role fence because, its comment says, nothing grants it — on the hosted project a cashier could adjust stock past the manager fence its callers hold; (5) email sign-up confirms itself, so a signed-in person is anyone with an email address, and the check needs the list of what she may execute, not only anon's half.
**Measured on the hosted database first (the catalog only, `supabase/pgtap/_walls.sql`, 2026-10-27 23:30 UTC): 96 breaches.** `anon` may execute **50** of the **60** functions in `public` outside extensions (`10.1`'s *27 of 35* counts the `security definer` ones a caller can call: 29 of 38 less three trigger functions) and holds every privilege on all **7** views; a signed-in person may execute **18** functions CI never grants her and write through all 7 views; **14** default grants open every new function, table and sequence. Base-table grants for the client roles are identical in both databases (23 tables); `catalogo` and `servicio` give them no access; the three storage buckets are private.
**The field rule, read before any revoke:** no build ever called one of the 18 — every mention in the history of `app/`, `site/`, `packages/` and `supabase/functions/` is a comment; the two RPCs a signed-out caller could reach in use, `catalog_template` and `collection_reaches`, have been asked only with a session since their hooks were written; nothing writes through a view; the Edge Function reads no table.
**What changed:** `0071` — the defaults for what `postgres` creates in `public` grant `anon` and `authenticated` nothing that executes, reads or writes (CI's already); `anon` executes nothing (a loop, so the platform's function is covered); the three functions PUBLIC carried keep `authenticated` by name; the 18 are taken from `authenticated`; on views `anon` holds nothing and `authenticated` select only; and the file refuses to commit if a wall it claims is still open. **`supabase/pgtap/_walls.sql`** — one query, one row per breach, holding the **35** functions a signed-in person may execute; CI asserts it as **F12** of `01_rls_coverage.sql`, and `5R-f-schema-deployed.sh` runs it on the hosted project as **group 7** (its falsifier: **15** fixtures, 5 new). `CLAUDE.md` and `supabase/README.md`: a function a client calls is named in that list in the same pull request.
**Looked at, on the local stack, in one transaction that rolled back** (it stops at `0068` and holds old suite leftovers, dropped inside it; `0070` applied inside it): the walls before `0071` — the three PUBLIC functions; after — none. As `anon`: `catalog_template`, `collection_reaches`, `auth_full_name`, an invoker view and `waste_reason_line` all refused. As an owner: `auth_full_name` and `adjust_stock_delta` refused; the starter catalog, the views, a product edit and a supplier edit allowed. ⚠️ **Falsified: without section 3's grant, that owner's product edit and supplier edit both failed** — *permission denied for function purging_workspace*. `01_rls_coverage.sql` **98 of 98** with `0071` and **97 of 98** without, F12 naming the three. `5R-f` on the hosted project before the deploy: red twice — `0071` not applied, and **96** walls. The check mutated three ways (breaches ignored, a failed answer ignored, a refused link read): each caught by its fixture.
**Decided on his behalf:** ⚠️ **the hosted defaults are changed by a migration** — functions, tables, sequences — which every later migration meets; it is CI's state already, and Supabase applies two of the three on 2026-10-30; a grant reverses it. **`service_role` is untouched.** **The two trigger functions PUBLIC carried keep `authenticated`**, kept as they were rather than proved unneeded. **A function a client calls is named in `_walls.sql`'s list in the same pull request** — one line per door, and CI goes red without it.
**Merged and deployed:** `de43fe5`, pull request 415 — the 11 jobs that could fire read by name (runs `40604354953` and `40604355043`), every step a success: *supabase db reset* applied `0071`, ran **7** pgTAP suites — `01_rls_coverage.sql` **98 of 98**, F12 *ok* — and **49** behavioural suites with no `not ok` in the log; the catalog-edit, prebuilt-catalog and provider contracts, which write as a signed-in manager, green; *app (node 22)* **2306** tests, the gate **18 groups** and its **36** fixtures, the rehearsal **13 cases** and **5 of 5**; *the documents still agree* (the handover **11 groups**, the handbook **5** and its **13** fixtures). `supabase db push` applied `0071` — its own check passing on the hosted project. ⚠️ **Then `5R-f`'s first real run went red on group 7 with the walls clean**: over the hosted path `supabase db query -o csv` prints a zero-row result as one blank line, no header, where the local path the draft was measured on prints the header. Fixed in pull request 416: the query always returns one `examined` row, so no row is no answer; the falsifier gains W6 (an empty answer) and W7 (no `examined` row) — **17** fixtures, four mutations each caught. **Then `5R-f` 7 of 7 (2026-10-28 00:00 UTC):** *the hosted project keeps the walls — no breach in 60 functions, 30 tables and views, 35 names on the list*. Read back there: the defaults for `postgres` in `public` grant `anon` and `authenticated` no EXECUTE and no read or write; `catalog_template`, `purging_workspace` and `record_sale` executable by a signed-in person and not by `anon`; `auth_full_name`, `adjust_stock_delta` and `rls_auto_enable` by neither.

**What the investigation measured (Android 16 emulator, every text box inventoried):** (a) ⚠️⚠️ **Android 15+ no longer shortens the screen for the keyboard** — Wera's window stayed 1080 × 2400 with it up (`adjust=resize` overridden by `EDGE_TO_EDGE_ENFORCED`; Wera targets API 36). Vender, Comprar and Merma assumed the opposite (*"Expo sets `softwareKeyboardLayoutMode: "resize"`"*), and no form made room at all; (b) on *Entrar* the password was dots only, with nothing to show it — most likely his user's complaint — and with the keyboard up *Acepto* and *Crear cuenta* sat under it, unreachable; on an iPhone, whose keyboard is taller, the password box itself is under it; (c) the postal code's number pad had no key to close it on an iPhone, the *Te lo compro* note's key only made new lines, and four boxes had no confirm key.

The note: ✓ that closes. ✓ on Ajustes' name and shop-name boxes, Comprar's cost and the price sheet. The counters: `comportamientoTeclado` — room made by the app on iOS and Android 15+, by the system on Android 14 and older — and on Android 15+ the room counted from where the counter starts (`useDesfaseTeclado`; it was short by the header, 145 px). ▶️ **2026-10-22 — `8g`: *CONOCE WERA*, HIS TWO FIXES — THE BAR FILLS WHERE HE CAN SEE IT, AND A CARD STAYS AS LONG AS ITS WORDS TAKE. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK.**
**His words:** ***"The time bar at the top is not moving so people don't know how much time left they have on each slide. Also make the time longer on each slide, it's too little to understand what the screen is about and read. Fix the whole thing and let me know when we have our final version for both Android and iOS."***
**The cause of the first:** the bar WAS filling — the whole current segment was drawn at 35 % opacity and its fill sat inside that dimming, the same white as its own track, so it filled unseen. ✅ **2026-10-20, night — EVERY REQUEST HAS A TIME LIMIT: 30 s, 60 s FOR THE CATALOG, 90 s FOR A PHOTO GOING UP. `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — with him: the first day, the last thing before the Android users.**
**His words:** ***"Go ahead with the request time limit before the Android users."***
**The estimate, written first:** `S` — one pure module and one line in the client. Read on the way in: React Native's `OkHttpClientProvider` builds Android's client with every timeout at zero; every request of the app's one client goes through its `fetch`, and supabase-js hands that `fetch` to the database, storage, the functions and the sign-in's renewal alike; photo downloads go through Expo's downloader, which has OkHttp's own 10 s defaults. **The limits are measured:** the largest answer the app receives is the catalog, **382 KB** for 841 products (read off the local gateway's log), about 31 s at 100 kbps — 60 s, with the catalog import; a photo is 1280 px at JPEG 0.7, 150–300 KB, and goes up slower — 90 s; everything else is a few KB — 30 s.
**What changed:** `@/lib/plazo` — `withLimit` (an `AbortController` per request, a caller's own signal still honoured, the timer cleared when the request settles), `limitFor` by path and method, and a request over its limit fails as an `AbortError` whose message says `Network request failed`, so `@/api/errors` and `@/auth/errors` read it as *Sin conexión*, `auth-js` keeps the session, the outbox retries it and Te lo compro holds its send; `@/lib/supabase` — `global: { fetch: withLimit(fetch) }`; `@/api/QueryProvider` — a read that ran out of time is not retried (`isTimedOut`).
**Measured on the Android emulator, with the local gateway paused so requests are accepted and never answered:** with a temporary log in the wrapper (removed before the commit), every request passed through it, the limit fired at **exactly 30 s** and the request was cancelled (*Fetch request has been canceled*). ⚠️⚠️ **And the first version was not enough:** failing as a `TypeError`, postgrest-js retried the read three more times by itself (1, 2, 4 s apart, 30 s each) and the data library retried that chain twice — a hung read took about **six minutes** to say so. Named `AbortError` — which postgrest-js never retries — and not retried by the data library, **Precios read *Sin conexión a internet. Intenta de nuevo en un momento.* at 38 s.** A sale rung up while the gateway hung landed 5 s after it returned. ⚠️ **What this could not show:** a link that drops packets silently — the hang behind the evening's stuck Te lo compro send — needs a firewall rule this machine does not allow; cutting the tunnel closes the connection instead, and the old client then fails at once too (measured: both landed 11 s after it returned). **Not measured:** iOS, whose own client has a 60 s default.
**Decided on his behalf:** the three limits above; a read that ran out of time is said at once and read again by itself on the reconnect, the return and the pull, rather than tried twice more; a write that ran out of time may still have landed — the outbox's writes and Te lo compro's are idempotent by their client ids, and creating a shop (`onboard_workspace`, no idempotency key) is the one write where a slow-but-landed answer followed by a second tap could make two, as a lost reply already could.

**The estimate, written first:** `L` — a dozen screens with one rule, plus a held write. **What changed:** **one rule** — a read with nothing to draw and no signal says *Se ve cuando haya señal*; a read holding data shows it whatever its last attempt said (`salesLine`, `takingsLine`, `documentsLine`, `pricesLine`, `costsLine`, `catalogLine`, `providerLine` and new selectors in `month`, `semana`, `productPage`, `providerPage`, `approvals`, `providers`, `collection`) — on Números (*Tu mes*, *Ventas*), Inicio (*Hoy*'s line, *Lo último*), *Tu semana*, *Lo último*, Precios, Costos, Solicitudes, Comprar's picker, the product page, a supplier's page, *Tu tienda*'s address and the catalog search; the discoveries and *la racha* wait until the six months are read. **Te lo compro** (`@/offline/teLoCompro`): offline the door is closed and says *Para pedir que pasemos necesitas internet*; a send tapped without signal is held (`networkMode: 'online'`, the one write that overrides `always`), the button says *Se envía cuando haya señal*, and it goes by itself; a lost link mid-send retries, a refusal does not; leaving drops it; photo paths and the draft id make a resend land once. **The pull** (`@/offline/jalar`, `useRefresh`, `@/ui/Jalar`): Inicio, *Lo último*, Números, Productos, Proveedores, Solicitudes — never the counters, a test reads them; offline it asks nothing and lets go at 600 ms; online it re-reads every active read, capped at 8 s. **The hints** (`@/offline/ensenar`, once per phone): *Wera funciona sin internet…* under the first *Venta registrada* (`Hecho`'s `note`, held 4.5 s), and *Sin internet: puedes seguir vendiendo* for the first outage. No mutation is written to the phone (`shouldDehydrateMutation: () => false`).
**Measured on the Android emulator** (local shop; the dev build cannot cold-start in airplane mode, so the server was cut first and airplane mode set after launch): Números offline — *Sin dato · Se ve cuando haya señal* on all three cards, no spinners, no *Se abren pronto*; *Lo último*, Precios, the product page (*Te cuesta*, *Esta semana*) and *Tu tienda*'s address — the sentence; **the Te lo compro door greyed with *Para pedir que pasemos necesitas internet*, and back to *Llegamos a tu zona* 4 s after the signal**; a request saved offline showed *Se envía cuando haya señal*, a second tap did nothing, and on the reconnect it landed once. ⚠️⚠️ **The first run of that found a defect and it is fixed:** the request landed (one row) but the screen never reached *Solicitud guardada* — the request list's read had hung on a link that died under it (Android's requests have no timeout), and an awaited `invalidateQueries` hands back that very fetch for a query with no data. The list is now re-read and not waited for; **re-run: *Solicitud guardada* 4 s after the signal, one new row.** The first outage read *Sin internet: puedes seguir vendiendo*, the second *Sin conexión a internet*; the first sale carried its line and the next did not; the pull drew its spinner on Productos and, offline, let go before 1.8 s. **Not measured:** iOS; Solicitudes, Costos, Comprar's picker and the supplier page offline (the rule and its tests are the same); a pull on the four screens added last.
**Decided on his behalf:** a held copy beats a failure everywhere, which changes a few ONLINE cases too (*Hoy*'s count, *Tu mes*, the Ventas chart, *Lo último*'s empty week, *Agregar*'s form now open after a failed re-read); discoveries and *la racha* hidden until known rather than saying the sentence twice; the month download fails fast offline (`always`); the product page's week bars hidden while waiting; Te lo compro offline overrides even a cached *outside the zone* or *no address* (its door to Ajustes cannot be tapped offline); online, a send whose link drops tries twice more 3 s apart, so *Sin conexión* arrives ~6 s later; leaving stops an online send before its next step; the hints are per phone, so a person already using Wera sees each once after this update.
**Left open, for him or a later round:** ⚠️ **Android requests have no timeout** — the cause of today's stuck send, and of any spinner on a link with no internet behind it; a timeout on the app's one client is the fix, and it touches every request, so it is its own round. Comprar's supplier picker can trap an iPhone offline when the list was never read (Android's back closes it). A phone on Wi-Fi with no internet counts as online (the signal reads connectivity, not reachability). Multi-step writes can still stop halfway on a dropping signal (a price change, *Nuevo producto*).

✅ **2026-10-20, late night — HOY COUNTS WHAT SHE SOLD, SENT OR NOT; THE RECONNECT TOAST WAITS FOR THE QUEUE; THE OFFLINE NOTICE NO LONGER GOES DEAF; MERMA WEARS A BASKET; THE LEGAL PAGES SAY MERMA (the report's C and F, its icon and its legal call — round 2 of 3). `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK — with him: round 3 (D, E and the first-day hints), then the first day, the last thing before the Android users.**
**His words:** ***"Do all the fixes, make sure everything is correct but you have permission to do everything is needed."***
**The estimate, written first:** `M` for this round — six pieces, all JavaScript or site text, no migration — and the rest of the report (`D`, `E`, the hints) as round 3, because the three together are `XL` and each round ships on its own. ⚠️⚠️ **Found while sizing, and it changed the order:** `Drain` restarts the connectivity monitor whenever the session object changes, and the monitor's stop ran `watchers.clear()` — so every reader that subscribed once at mount (the offline notice, the reconnect toast, and `onlineManager` from the round before) went deaf after the first token refresh. **Pull request 369's update was held for it** and ships with this round.
**What changed:** `@/lib/connectivityMonitor` keeps its watchers across a restart and announces each drain (`onDrain`: how it stopped, how many landed). `@/offline/notice` — a reconnect ARMS the toast (`awaiting`); it fires when a drain reports `drained` having landed at least one write since; an outage with nothing written stays silent. `@/api/today` — `rowsOfToday` (only a read made on this device day is today's: yesterday's figure no longer sits under *Hoy* past midnight) and `takingsWithUnsent` (the server's rows plus the queued sales it does not hold, matched by id, so a landed sale is counted once in the render that brings it; an unknown server figure stays the dash). `useTakingsWithUnsent` in `@/offline/useUnsent` feeds *Hoy* and *la racha*; Inicio's *Lo último* card merges the unsent sales, deduped by id, with no mark. `['today', 'takings']` is the eighth persisted key, safe behind the day guard. A landed write-off invalidates Merma's list. Merma's tab, door and *Lo último* rows wear `basket-minus-outline` (*Tirar* keeps its trash can — it is the act). The aviso and the deletion page say *merma* (nine places), the aviso's phone copy now reads *Al cerrar sesión se borra esa copia.* — true since the last round — and both pages are dated 20 October.
**Measured on the Android emulator:** offline, a $15.50 sale took *Hoy* from **$34.50 · 1 venta to $50 · 2 ventas** and topped the card (before: unchanged); on the reconnect the sale landed and *Hoy* **stayed at 2 ventas** through 75 s of readings every 5 s — never counted twice — and the toast showed; **an outage with nothing written passed with no toast** (before: always). **Measured against his folders** (`~/RetailerToolDev/wera-fotos`, matched by `codigo` in any giro's folder): of the 1,413 default products, **1,161 have a photo**; **137 wait** with the aisles he left for later (`cat-alcohol` 120, `cat-tabaco` 7, `cat-mascotas` 10 — two of these, the loose *croquetas para perro* and *para gato*, sit under *A granel* in his folders but are pet food, so they wait rather than go); **115 go**. One file named by a download in *Higiene personal* was looked at: *Papel higiénico Pétalo*, 32 rolls — renamed to its code and kept. ⚠️ **And three of his photos were AVIF files saved with no extension** (*Atún Dolores con verduras*, *Tostadas Charras*, *Jamón de pierna*): the session's first count read them as missing and put them on the list; the upload named them as *a format it does not read*, so they were given `.avif`, uploaded, and taken off it.
**The photos:** `docs/runbooks/catalog-photos.sh --linked` (his word, *"let's add the photos now"*) — each scaled to 720 and 160 px on white, no metadata, into the bucket `catalogo`, and `catalogo.product.image_path` set: **1,007 new and 155 already up from `8o`; the hosted catalog now names 1,161 photos.** 547 are under 720 px (soft on a product's page, sharp in the lists); 21 pictures are shared by two products, which he says is intended. 0 failed.
**The removal:** the 115 rows came out of `supabase/catalog/products/*.csv` and `build.mjs --write solo_con_foto` generated `0065_catalogo_solo_con_foto.sql`, a snapshot of the whole template (1,298 products) that never writes `image_path`. ⚠️ **The session first hand-wrote a delete** — guarded to refuse any count but its own, which caught a comma lost between its last two codes — **and CI refused it**: the starter-catalog step checks that the newest catalog migration is exactly what the CSVs generate (`9b`), and a hand-written one leaves the CSVs still listing what it deleted. No shop's copy is touched: `template_code` has no foreign key. Suite `0065` (6 checks); the starter-catalog, catalog-origin, photos and main-giro suites unchanged and green locally.
**Decided on his behalf:** the two loose croquetas wait with Mascotas; the Pétalo file was renamed rather than skipped. ⚠️ **Staples are among the 115** — *Bolillo*, *Telera*, *Crema ácida*, *Azúcar refinada a granel*, the two harinas — removed as he ruled; any comes back with its photo by a migration that re-inserts its row (`0045`–`0052` hold them).
**Merged and deployed:** `36863ff`, pull request 353 — the 11 jobs its paths fire, each *success* and read by name on its third run (the first two ran the hand-written delete and were red on the starter-catalog step, as above): the generator's check *exactly what the CSVs generate (45 tags, 42 families, 1298 products)*, 44 behavioural suites with `0065`'s 6, the starter catalog's 37 and the photos' 17. **`supabase db push` applied `0065` to the hosted project and `5R-f` read every merged migration there; the hosted catalog holds 1,298 products, 1,161 with a photo and 137 waiting, and the shops' 2,278 copies are untouched.** No app change and no update to ship: a phone reads the catalog and the photos from the server on its next catalog read.

✅ **2026-10-20 — `8g`: NÚMEROS OPENS ON A CENTRE — TU MES IN THREE COLOURS, A SHOP'S FIRST WEEK, AND *DESCUBRE* (ROUND 1 OF 4). `8g` IS STILL THE NEXT TASK, WITH HIM.**
**His rulings:** asked where to start (Proveedores or Números), ***"Numeros"***. Shown A/B/C for what Números says first, he asked for more: ***"I want numbers that are clean and helpful yes, but also appealing and that encourage users to see their numbers from the very first moment. We might be able to 'teach' them how to visualize and understand inflation, stationality and other things, but from the first week let's create something that can be interesting to them … covering the basics for them to check their 'monthly report' of sales, purchases and waste but also something to nourish the curiosity."*** The canvas (*Números · una propuesta para que den ganas de verlos*) drew the centre, *Tu mes*, a shop's first week, six discoveries (hours, best day, the quincena, the average ticket, inflation with her own products, the year in squares) and a Monday story. Asked ***"What do you suggest then?"***, the session's suggestion — the centre, built in four rounds (1: the centre, *Tu mes*, the first week and two discoveries; 2: quincena and the average ticket; 3: inflation and the year; 4: *Tu semana*); **tips only about her own patterns, never an order quantity or a supplier**; **the same Números for everyone** (no new rule: a cashier already reads purchases, `0040`, and write-off quantities, `0041`); **pesos and words, no percentages** — ***"Let's do."***
**Estimate, written before building:** `L`, one sitting — a data module with three new reads (a month of delivery headers, a month of write-offs with their lines, four weeks of sale headers, plus each kind's reversals since the month opened), a contract and falsifier for them, a restructured screen, its words and tests; the sales read Números already held serves the month, its comparison and the best weekday. **It held.** What it missed: Inicio and Lo último each drew an identical small-capitals label, so the third copy became `src/ui/Rotulo.tsx` (`R14`); and the gate refused two ways of making a float (`R5`) in the first draft.
**What changed:** `numeros.tsx` opens on the centre — ‹ the month › (six months back, as far as the sales read reaches); for a shop under a week old, *TU PRIMERA SEMANA · Llevas N días con Wera* and seven dots; **TU MES**: *VENDISTE* in green with *$405 más que en los mismos días de septiembre* (the month under way against the SAME days of the last; a finished month against the whole one; none for a shop that was not selling through those days; a fall in ink with a down arrow, never red), opening the sales page — the old screen, unchanged — on that month; *COMPRASTE* in blue with *2 entregas · Don Chuy el que más*; *SE TIRÓ* in berenjena, *8 piezas* (pieces, kilos and litres apart, never added) and *Caducado, lo que más*; **DESCUBRE**, cards that slide and snap — *¿A qué hora vendes más? De 7 a 9 de la noche* (the two local hours that sold the most money in four weeks, once there are 20 sales) and *Tu mejor día: Los jueves* (the best average over eight weeks, of the days that sold, today left out, in whole pesos), each with one line of what it is for; **SE ABREN PRONTO**, a locked card in amber with its progress (*Te faltan 17 ventas*, *En 5 días*) for each not yet open; and the month's download, following the centre's month. Pure: `@/api/month` (`standing`, `monthSales`, `monthPurchases`, `monthWaste`, `hoursOf`, `bestDay`, `shopAge`); reads: `documentsBetween`, `reversalsSince` (`@/api/calls`), `useMonthReport`, `useSaleHours`.
**Evidence — the code:** Vitest **2026 passed, 68 files** (`numeros-mes.test.ts`, 22 new, the hours under a pinned zone); the gate 18 of 18; `docs/checks/8g-numeros-contract.sh` **7 groups** over a real PostgREST (the app's columns, figures as strings, every void named, a cashier reading the owner's month write-off lines included, a stranger reading none) and its falsifier **5 fixtures**, each red for its reason — the first one the embed from manager-only `waste_line`, a 200 with `[]` in her hand. **On the Android emulator**, Luz's local shop: at three days old, the first week, *Tu mes* and both discoveries locked in amber; given seven weeks of history (local only), the comparison with September, *8 piezas · Caducado*, both discoveries open and snapping, *Vendiste* opening the sales page on October, Android's back returning to the centre. ⚠️ **The contract's first run** answered 400 to all three voids and every run since has been green (five); the void's body now prints if it happens again.
**Decided on his behalf:** the month under way compared with the same days of the last (else *menos* every month until its last day); a fall said in ink, not red; only *Vendiste* is a door this round (a month of deliveries or write-offs has no screen yet, and a chevron into a seven-day list would be the wrong room); a discovery shows only when it has enough to be true (20 sales; a week) and waits in amber until then; the hours measured in money, not tickets; the best day averaged over the days that sold, in whole pesos; the top supplier named only when there is more than one, under its stored name (*Genérico*); the download lost its own month picker and follows the centre's; the first-week card for seven days.
**Merged and shipped:** `5d6303f`, pull request 351 — the 11 jobs its paths fire, each *success* and read by name: the data layer's new Números contract 7 groups and its 5 fixtures (no void answered 400 on CI), Precios, the month export; the app's Vitest **2026 in 68 files**, the gate 18 of 18 and its 36 fixtures; the 43 behavioural suites. Over the air as iOS group `78be5096`, Android `8ee13635`, *Números: tu mes, tu primera semana y Descubre (8g)*. No migration.
**Next:** his phone, then round 2 (the quincena and the average ticket), then Proveedores and the first day.

## Step 5R — the release path, and it is now a STEP (§2.11)

⚠️⚠️ **PROMOTED FROM PROSE TO TASKS ON 2026-09-21, ON THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION, AND THE
TARGET CHANGED WITH IT.** §2.11 carries the release path as a paragraph and says *"it
is not a step because it blocks nothing"*. **That was true when the pilot's output was
a measurement. It is false now that the pilot's output is also a thing shown to
investors and partners** — the owner ruled on 2026-09-21 that the app ships to **both
public stores**, not to TestFlight and Play internal testing alone.

⚠️ **The recommendation on file was internal distribution only**, on the grounds that
App Store review sits inside §5's three-day observation window. **The owner overrode
it with a reason the recommendation had not weighed: a public listing is evidence to a
partner, and this pilot exists to raise money as well as to measure completeness.**
Recorded here rather than argued again.

⚠️ **§2.11's acceptance test survives the change and is not optional:** an over-the-air
JS-only fix shipped to a real device in under an hour, proven **before** pilot day.
Public review governs binaries; it does not govern OTA updates, so this is what keeps
a 48-hour review out of the observation window.

⚠️⚠️ **THE IDS ARE `5R-x` AND `5P-x` RATHER THAN `R1`/`P1`, AND THAT IS A CORRECTION MADE
THE SAME DAY.** They were first written `R1`–`R5` and `P1`–`P3`, and **every one of the
eight collided with a row that already existed** — `R1`–`R13` are `docs/CONVENTIONS.md`'s
RULE names (`R12` and `R13` are cited across the plan and read by
`conventions-gate.sh`), and `P1`–`P3` are falsification fixtures in the `5a-iv` tables.
⚠️ **A grep for `R3` returned a store gate and an empty-env-var fixture**, which is the
one-identifier-two-meanings defect this file has recorded several times. The `5` prefix
also says something true: §2.11 puts the release path *"running in parallel from 5a"*.

## Step 7 — Números, and the read the tier-2 business runs on

⚠️ **WRITTEN 2026-09-21. §3 carries step 7 as the single word *"Números"*.** It is the
surface the BI half of the business model is sold on, and every view it reads is
already applied: `0031` purchases and gross revenue, `0032` price over time, `0033`
the month export. ⚠️ **Área 9 settled its content on 2026-09-14** — *"charts and
tables about their transactions … it doesn't have to be very robust nor
sophisticated for now"* — so this is three questions, not thirty.

| Task | What it is | Size | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **7c** | ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT AM I THROWING AWAY — and the honest half is QUANTITY.** §2.9 records the cost half as broken under C8.6: on a despiece the numerator reads a shortfall lot's cost of **zero** and the denominator is purchases of a product the shop buys whole, so **the headline is 0 over 0**. Quantity by product is sound and is what ships. ⚠️ **Pricing waste properly is its own modelling decision and is not folded in here** | `M` | ✅ **UNGATED as of 2026-09-27** — ~~`6a` — there is no waste data until that screen exists~~. ⚠️ **Re-pointed from `6a` to `6a-i` and then cleared, because `6a` split that day and *a gate naming a row nobody can take is a gate nobody can clear*** — the trap `5h`'s and `5h.5`'s splits both recorded. `6a-i` closed 2026-09-27, so the screen exists. ⚠️ **What it still wants is DATA rather than a row**: a pilot shop that has actually thrown things away for a while |
| **7e** | ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT IS AT RISK OF BECOMING WASTE — DERIVED, NOT TYPED IN. REWRITTEN 2026-09-21 WHEN THE OWNER RULED OUT CAPTURING EXPIRY DATES.** ~~stock inside its last days, read from a captured `expiry_date`~~. **Three numbers per variant, all of them computed from records the shop already produces:** **shelf age** (how long the stock on hand has been sitting — `stock_batch.received_at`, which is `not null` and always present, against `batch_balance`); **days of cover** (what is on hand ÷ recent daily velocity, over `product_velocity_daily`, which `0013`/`0014` already ship); and **observed time-to-waste** (for each variant, how many days typically pass between receiving and writing off — read from `waste` against the batches it consumed). ⚠️ **A variant with more days of cover than its own observed time-to-waste is the buy-it-now candidate**, and that is the tier-2 signal without a single new field. ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT IT COSTS, AND IT IS THE REASON THE DEFERRED DECISION IS NOT FREE: a derived shelf life NEEDS WEEKS OF WASTE RECORDS BEFORE IT SAYS ANYTHING.** A typed expiry date answers on day one; this answers once the pilot has run long enough to have thrown things away. **That is a pilot-duration cost, not an engineering one**, and it is the owner's deliberate trade — ~~see ⛔ DECISIONS OWED, área 7~~ (ruled; its record is in the archive, not in the block) | `M` | ⚠️ **`6a-i` and `5g` — RE-POINTED 2026-09-27**, because `6a` split that day and a gate naming a split parent is one nobody can clear. ✅ **Both screens now exist** (`5g-ii` 2026-09-25, `6a-i` 2026-09-27). ⚠️⚠️ **What is left in this gate is TIME and it cannot be hurried**: a derived shelf life needs weeks of waste records, and the clock started 2026-09-27 |

same instrument.

## Step 8 — from alpha to beta: the cleaning day (2026-09-29) and the design day (2026-09-30)

**Written 2026-09-29, after midnight, at the owner's request** — *"make a diagnosis of the current
status of the way you're progressing, the graph and any other thing we leverage. And establish a one
day plan for us to tidy it along with its own Cleaning Prompt."* The prompt is in `docs/HANDBOOK.md`,
beside the main prompt, because that is the file he reads. **This section is the plan; the handbook
holds only the prompt and the pointer.**

### The diagnosis — every number measured 2026-09-29 00:30 CST, and the tool that gave it

**What is healthy — the product.**
- `npm --prefix app test`: **1,719 tests across 52 files, all green.** The last 40 CI runs
  (`gh run list`) were all green except one `db` failure on the `5P-a` branch, fixed before merge.
- `5R-f-schema-deployed.sh`: **6 of 6** — all 41 migrations applied to the hosted project.
- The hosted shop (`supabase db query --linked`): **24 sales, 10 deliveries, 2 write-offs,
  1 supplier, 28 products.** Every screen reads and writes that database; no screen runs on sample
  data (checked by searching `app/src/app` for mock, fake, sample or TODO: none).
- **The owner calls it an alpha, and that is accurate.** Selling, buying, waste, catalog, suppliers,
  corrections, Números, Precios, the month export and offline selling are built and connected.

**What is unhealthy — the process has outgrown the product** ([[process-must-not-outgrow-product]]
in the assistant's memory says he trades rigor for speed when a guard protects a document, not a shop).
- **The documents** (`wc -lc`): `docs/PLAN.md` **5,517 lines / 821 KB** — about 200k tokens, more than
  a session can read; `## Position` was at **1,298 of its 1,400 cap**. The archive is **11 files /
  18,955 lines**. `docs/HANDBOOK.md` is **1,193 lines / 263 KB** for a reader who is not a developer,
  and its catch-up contradicts itself (§2 mentions *"one of the four parked decisions"*, §3 says there
  are none; *What you are building* still calls Desperdicio an empty tab). `CLAUDE.md` **35 KB** +
  `app/CLAUDE.md` **29 KB** load into sessions, and most of it is the history of corrections to its own
  numbers. The warning sign appears **994 times** in the plan, **186** in the handbook and **70** in
  `CLAUDE.md` (`grep -c`) — when everything is flagged, nothing is.
- **The checks**: **75 shell scripts, 27,177 lines** in `docs/checks/`, against **42,483** lines of
  app source and **18,554** of app tests. A large share hold documents in agreement with each other,
  and `split-coverage` with its falsifier takes about six minutes.
- **The graph** (`graphify-out/graph.json`): **4,349 nodes, 8,592 edges, every one `_origin: ast`**,
  current as of `8d8758c` (built the same minute it merged). **1,500 of the nodes are `docs/`** —
  headings of the plan, mostly noise for a code question. No semantic layer (no API key), so it answers
  *where*, never *what was decided*. **34 dated snapshot folders, 105 MB**, nothing reads them.
- **Git**: **198 remote branches** — squash-merged PR heads nobody deleted — and **9 stale local**.
- **The assistant's memory**: **85 files**. Some describe one-off instruments (the sealed emulator,
  the 8-day reading) that expire with the dates block.

**What actually stands between the alpha and a beta — distribution, not code.**
- The app is installed with a free Apple account: **it stops opening every 7 days** (next: 4 October,
  14:28 UTC) and only this Mac can reinstall it. ⚠️ **No longer true since 2026-10-09**: the paid
  account signs the build for a year (see the dates block). A second person's phone cannot live like that.
  `5R-a` (Apple Developer Program + Play Console, **his** action) and `5R-b` (EAS / TestFlight) fix it.
- `5R-c` account deletion (a store requirement, ~~still needs a decision~~ **ruled and built
  2026-10-09, `0053`**) and `5R-d` the *aviso de privacidad* are the other two store gates. **No CI compiles the native app.**
- **Design readiness is unmeasured** — nobody has counted where colours, type sizes and animations
  live, or what the conventions gate will refuse when they change. That is `8e`.

### The day, in order — one row per session, the Cleaning Prompt takes the next one

⚠️ *(The cleaning day, 2026-09-29, is over; every row but `8g` and the parked `8h` is done. What follows was written for that day.)* ⚠️ **Nothing a shopkeeper sees changes today**: no migration, no screen, no access rule. Each row
comes out **smaller** and says by how much. **Order matters**: `handbook-agreement.sh` looks up the
handbook's task rows in `docs/PLAN.md` itself, NOT in the corpus, so the handbook sheds its rows
before the plan archives them.

| Task | What it is | Size | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **8a** | ✅ **DONE 2026-09-29** — 1,229 lines / 265 KB to 299 / 16 KB; the old file moved whole to `docs/plan/archive/handbook/handbook-through-2026-09-29.md` (a subfolder: in `docs/plan/archive/` itself `plan-corpus.sh` would read its task rows as second copies and `split-coverage` goes red — measured). **The handbook, rewritten for its reader.** One short file: what the app does today, what is on his phone, what is waiting on him, how a session goes (both prompts), the dates. The stale catch-up goes; the history moves to `docs/plan/archive/handbook-through-2026-09-29.md` — a MOVE. ## Step 9 — the starter catalog by giro (planned 2026-10-01, with the owner)

**The ask, in his words:** *"create a default catalog that the users can get access to depending on the
business they run, this will allow them to import products in bulk depending on which business or
categories they're interested in … for both the onboarding stage and the additional steps"*, for **Pollería,
Carnicería, Verdulería, Frutería, Abarrotes, Cremería and Materias Primas**. *"There might be intersections
between catalogs and each product could have tags … each product is intended to have it's picture at some
point and once the user imports the products, he should be able to have access to them offline as well. We
don't need to enrich the front end for now in terms of filters or tags, but the feature should be enabled with
minimum controls to design UI later on."*

**What is already settled and not reopened:** ADR-035 §2.9 — a shop STARTS from a catalog we maintain, the
rows are COPIED into the workspace, never referenced across tenants; `0042` minted `is_prebuilt` and the fence
for exactly this import. **Offline is already solved for imported rows**: they are ordinary `product_variant`
rows and reach the phone through the persisted `['catalog','variants']` read. **Pictures stay C8.14/C8.15**:
this step adds the column and nothing else.

**The design, and the decisions made for the owner** (each reported in its PR):
1. **The template lives in a schema of its own, `catalogo`, that PostgREST does not expose**, reached only through
   two `security definer` RPCs in `public` — `catalog_template()` (read) and `import_catalog()` (copy). Putting it
   in `public` would widen the RLS guard's ONE exemption (`unit`, F3/F5); a private schema leaves the guard as it is.
2. **Giro is a tag kind, not a table.** Overlaps are one product carrying several tags (*Huevo*: Abarrotes,
   Cremería, Pollería), so a later giro's session tags an existing code rather than duplicating it.
3. ⚠️ **`product_variant.template_code` — cheap now, expensive later.** Without it an imported row can never be
   traced to its template again: no picture assignment, no tags on a shop's products, no "already imported".
4. **No prices in the template.** An imported product reads *sin precio* until the shop prices it.
5. **A re-import, or a name the shop already has, is skipped silently** (users don't do bookkeeping).
6. **Content is authored as one CSV per giro and GENERATED into a numbered migration** (`9b`); corrections are new
   migrations, so the append-only rule holds.

| Task | What it is | Size | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **9a** | ✅ **DONE 2026-10-01** — see the status entry. **`0044`: the template schema and the import.** `catalogo.tag`, `.family`, `.product` (with `image_path`, nullable), `.product_tag`; one dimension per template family, enforced by trigger (C8.5, which the shop's own tables do not enforce); `product_variant.template_code` with a per-workspace partial unique index; `catalog_template()` and `import_catalog(workspace, tags, exclude)`; a behavioural suite (an import as manager, the staff refusal under `set local role authenticated`, a second import importing nothing, a name collision skipped, a second workspace untouched); the ADR §2.9 paragraph and the README row. **No content** — the template is empty until `9c` | `M` | Nothing — the design was planned with the owner |
| **9b** | ✅ **DONE 2026-10-01** — see the status entry. **The authoring format and its generator.** `supabase/catalog/<giro>.csv` and `build.mjs`: refuses an unknown unit, a family across two dimensions, a duplicate code or name, an undeclared tag — with the reason — and writes the upsert migration | `S` | `9a` — the generator writes into its tables |
| **9c** | ✅ **DONE 2026-10-01** — `0045`, 24 products; see the status entry. **Content session — Pollería, with the owner.** First because the pilot shop sells chicken. ## Step 10 — the ecosystem: Wera's pilot version, Wera Cocina, Itacate and the shared backend (opened 2026-10-27)

**What it is:** two new apps in this repository, on the one database — **Wera Cocina** for fondas (reserve
shops' surplus offers; publish their own surplus meals, *itacates*) and **Itacate** for neighbours (reserve an
itacate nearby, collect it in person with a 3-digit code) — and first, the pilot version of Wera itself.
Buyers collect and pay sellers in person; no app collects, holds or pays out a price. No couriers.

**Lanes:** Wera · Backend · Cocina · Itacate. One task per session, as everywhere in this plan.

⚠️ **A task in this step reads `~/RetailerToolDev/wera-vision/CLAUDE.md` first** — the owner's private notes,
outside this repository, which hold the build plan, his rulings and the reasoning. **Nothing from them is copied
here**: no strategy, fees, prices or research — only what the code needs.

outside the pilot region, *Itacate aún no llega a tu zona*; a declined or approximate location gives her colonia from the pilot's list instead (D). ⚠️ Her exact position never leaves her phone, and the square travels in the request's body, never its URL, and is never kept. ⚠️ **Its *pilot region*: Wera Cocina's search covers Mexico City and the State of Mexico since `10.4f` (his ruling of 2026-11-02) — ✅ **ruled 2026-11-04, night: Itacate's region is that same zone.** **Kitchens with nothing on offer now** (his ruling of 2026-11-01, late): a kitchen that published in the last 14 days stays on the map and the list as a faded pin, its card saying when it usually publishes — *Suele publicar entre semana, hacia las 17:00* once 3 or more publications in 4 weeks show a pattern, otherwise *Publicó ayer* or *Publicó el martes* — and leaves after 14 silent days; ## The other edges — what the pilot must LEARN, and it builds nothing

⚠️⚠️ **WRITTEN 2026-09-21 BECAUSE IT WAS MISSING, AND THE OWNER HAD TO ASK TWICE.**
His brief of 2026-09-21 had five numbered points; the realignment earlier that day
answered four of them and **silently dropped 4.5** — *"understand the needs for the
other edges (Monitoring, Couriers, Restaurants) to ask for more funding."* He then
asked, in terms, whether the prompt had been read. It had not been read closely
enough. This section is that point, and it is also the answer to his point 1 —
*"the plan … doesn't have the focus of continuous integration for the other things I
mentioned."*

⚠️ **NOTHING HERE IS A TASK AND NOTHING HERE SHIPS.** It is a register: what the pilot
must **observe** so that the next round's engineering can be specified from evidence
instead of from imagination. Every answer below comes out of WhatsApp, a spreadsheet
and a notebook — **the owner already has the retailers and one courier**, so the
observations can start before the app can capture anything.

⚠️⚠️ **THE ONE ARCHITECTURAL FACT THAT SHOULD NOT ARRIVE AS A SURPRISE LATER: every
edge below is a SECOND TENANCY MODEL.** This schema has `staff`, `manager` and
`owner`, and all three live *inside one shop*; RLS is built so that one workspace can
never read another's rows, and there is a test suite whose only job is proving it. **A
courier who sees several shops' pickups, or a restaurant that sees a retailer's
surplus, is the exact inverse of that** — see §6's *"cross-workspace analytics schema"*
and *"collection-partner notifications"*, the two deferrals that touch this. **It is
not a screen on this app. It is the largest single decision the funding round buys**,
and the register below is what makes that decision cheap when it comes.

| Edge | What the pilot must come back with | Why it cannot be specified now |
|---|---|---|
| **Couriers** | Runs completed per day by one person; minutes per pickup and per drop; the route actually walked or driven; **cost per run against gross margin per run**; how often a pickup fails and why; whether anything needed cold chain; how the courier is paid and settled | **Capacity is the whole scaling model.** Every projection for 10 couriers multiplies a number nobody has measured once. One courier for three weeks produces it |
| **Restaurants** | What they will actually buy and what they refuse; **the discount that closes a sale**; how much lead time they need and by what hour of the day; typical order size; repeat rate across weeks; who in the kitchen decides; payment terms they expect | **A marketplace is worthless if the demand side is thin**, and nothing in the retailer's data can tell you what a chef will buy. Four or five relationships over a month answers it |
| **Retailers** *(supply side)* | The discount they will accept to move stock **before** it is waste; how much notice they need; whether they will hold something aside; **and the acquisition question — did the waste offer bring them in, and did they stay for the numbers?** | This is the hook's central claim and it is currently an assumption. The app measures whether they RECORD; only conversation measures whether they SELL |
| **Monitoring** | ⚠️ **AMBIGUOUS IN THE BRIEF AND DELIBERATELY NOT GUESSED AT.** Two readings: (a) the OPERATOR console — the owner watching which shops have surplus today, what moved, which runs happened; or (b) SYSTEM monitoring — uptime, failed writes, dead letters, whether a shop stopped recording. **Both are real needs and they are different products** | ⚠️ **Reading (b) partly exists already and nobody has said so:** §2.10 specifies a nightly check, `failed_write` is a vendor-side surface by §2.8, and `5c-iv`'s dead-letter banner is its merchant-facing half. **Reading (a) is the operator console and is genuinely unbuilt.** ⚠️⚠️ **This is a question for the owner, and it is NOT in ⛔ DECISIONS OWED because it blocks nothing today** — it blocks the funding ask, not the pilot |
| **Neighbours** *(Itacate's list — his answers of 2026-11-05)* | Whether neighbours ask for any of these — and what would bring each back: **a line when a window is about to end** (*Termina en 15 min* — a neighbour who missed a window); **a filter**, by the six tags, by diet or by allergy (a neighbour who asks for *sin cerdo* or *vegetariano* — *vegetariano* is not one of the tags); **a favourite kitchen, *Seguir*** (a neighbour who keeps going back to one kitchen and asks to be told when it publishes); **a *Nueva* badge** (once some kitchens are not new); and which of *Ahora*, *Más cerca* and *Más barato* they use, as far as they say | Each is his choice on `10.5-iv-a-iii`'s page (https://claude.ai/artifact/M5kESDfKVSy3mFEyT3g7dy) to wait for neighbours rather than guess: a filter promises more than a fonda's own tags keep, and a bag carries none; *Seguir* needs a rule about who sees who follows whom, and usually a push; *Nueva* means nothing while every kitchen is new. Never, by the same answers: the window that starts soonest, the biggest saving, the fewest left, the most regular kitchen, the newest dish, and — a paid call that sends her location to Google — minutes on foot |

**How to use this register.** Add to it as the pilot runs — one line per observation,
dated. ⚠️ **It is deliberately a REGISTER and not a status board**: nothing here has a
state, a size or a gate, because nothing here is being built. When the funding ask is
written, this section is the evidence; when the next architecture decision is taken,
this section is the input it was missing.

⚠️ **The honest sequencing point, repeated here because it is the one that decides
whether the money arrives:** three of the four rows above can be filled in **starting
this week**, with no app at all, and none of them gets faster by waiting for the build.
The engineering queue and the observation register should run in parallel, or the
three-to-four weeks of operating evidence lands *after* the software instead of
alongside it.

---

## Working agreement

One task per session. Before starting, estimate difficulty. Update this file when a
task closes — a plan that lags the code is the failure ADR-035 §9 names.

⚠️⚠️ **AMENDED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-24 — AN `M` OR AN `L` IS ONE SITTING NOW:**
*"we'll make an ammendment now that we have Claude Max, if a task is M or L, let's do
it at once, we have enough usage and space to do it."* **The old rule split anything
large; this one splits `XL` and above.**

⚠️ **THE PREMISE OF THE OLD RULE WAS A BUDGET AND IT HAS CHANGED, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS
AN AMENDMENT AND NOT A LOOSENING.** *"so the work survives a usage limit or a context
clear"* — the splitting was never about the work being clearer in halves, it was about
half-finished work being lost. A plan is a poor place to carry a subscription tier's
constraint after the tier has moved.

⚠️⚠️ **WHAT DOES NOT CHANGE, AND IT IS THE HALF THAT PAID FOR ITSELF: THE SIZING PASS
STAYS.** Sixteen of the seventeen splits in this project were preceded by an estimate
that **found something the row did not say**, and the finding — not the split — is what
those sessions were worth. `5f`'s sizing found the cart store §2.11 had already chosen,
the quantity control's contradiction with the ADR, and a commit payload that would have
hard-coded a gross/net identity. `5f-iii`'s, the same morning, found that the basket
sheet is the screen §2.5 rule 5 is written about. **None of those needed a split to be
worth taking; they needed somebody to look before writing.** So: estimate first, write
down what the estimate found, and then build the whole row.

⚠️⚠️ **AND A SPLIT MAY STILL HAPPEN AT `M` OR `L` — FOR A REASON THAT IS NOT SIZE, AND
IT MUST SAY SO IN ITS OWN WORDS.** Three survive the amendment, because none of them is
about how long a sitting is:

1. **A GATE.** Half the row is blocked on a decision the owner owes or an ADR amendment
   he has not made, and the other half is not. `5f-ii` was gated and `5f-i` was not;
   splitting is what let the unblocked half ship the same day.
2. **TWO FAILURE CLASSES IN ONE ROW, one of which nothing here can see.** This is
   `5c-iv`'s, `5d-iv`'s, `5e-iii`'s and `5f-iii`'s argument, and it is the strong one:
   when a row carries work whose mistakes cost a glance AND work whose mistakes are
   silent — the ledger, the queue — one row means **the invisible half rides in on the
   back of the visible one**, and it is reviewed as though somebody had looked at it.
3. **THE DEFERRAL TEST BELOW**, which already splits a row whose look-questions cannot
   be answered yet from the half that can be built today.

⚠️ **A size-only split at `M` or `L` is now the thing to refuse**, and a session that
wants one is asking for the old rule back. ⚠️ **`XL` still splits on size alone** —
`5f` listed twenty deliverables across a store, two screens and a question the owner
owed, and no premise about usage made that one row.

Every schema claim must be traceable to a migration CI has applied. A file is not
evidence; a green CI run is.

⚠️⚠️ **AND ITS COUNTERPART, RULED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-22: A ROW WHOSE ONLY
INSTRUMENT IS HIS EYES WAITS UNTIL THERE IS SOMETHING TO LOOK AT.**
*"If it's better for us to build something that needs to be looked at after we have
what's needed to actually look at it, let's keep it deferred."*

`R9` already routes a deliverable no check can see to the task that can see it. This
is the ordering half of the same sentence, and it was ruled the day `5c-iv-b`
demonstrated the gap: that task shipped a banner whose look is fenced out of every
suite by §2.11 — so the owner's phone is its whole instrument — **and nothing in this
app enqueues anything until `5f`, so the outbox is always empty and the banner never
draws.** It was reported as *"goes to the owner's phone"* when it could not.

**The test is two questions, and BOTH must be yes to defer:**

1. Is every judgement left in this row one no check here can make — rendering,
   navigation, layout (§2.11)?
2. Is the state it draws **unreachable today**, because the thing that produces that
   state has not been built?

⚠️ **One yes is not enough, and conflating them would stop the wrong work.** `5d` and
`5e` draw the catalog, which already exists: they answer *yes* to (1) and *no* to (2),
and they are takeable the moment they come up — the owner can open the app and look.
⚠️ **A row with real arithmetic in it is not deferred for the half that has a suite**;
`5c-iv-b`'s pricing was falsifiable here and 29 assertions read it. **What defers is
the part only an eye can settle.**

⚠️ **This is NOT a reason to revert `5c-iv-b`.** It is merged, green and correct, and
throwing away checked work buys nothing when the alternative is the same code written
later. **What it changes is where the look-questions are answered**: they move onto
`5f`'s row rather than being put to the owner blind, and they are listed there.
